# isaxcheck allowlist — justified residual decoder-metadata disagreements.
#
# Author: Maccoy Merrell
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0-or-later
#
# isaxcheck compares the decode metadata the ChampSim Tracer actually
# consumes (cap_disas_raw_detail() from disas/capstone.c — Capstone plus
# every correction the boundary applies) against the LLVM MC layer, over an
# exhaustive sweep of the opcode-bearing encoding space.  Anything it reports
# that is not listed here fails the gate.
#
# Format:  <isa> <signature key>
#          `#` starts a comment; `*` in the key is a glob wildcard.
# Signature keys are `CLASS mnemonic [qualifier]`, where the qualifier for
# the register classes is the difference set with register numbers collapsed
# to `#` (so `+r#` means "some general-purpose register", `+za` means "the
# SME accumulator tile").
#
# EVERY ENTRY BELOW NAMES: what the disagreement is, why it is not a tracer
# defect, and how to check whether it can be deleted.  An entry that cannot
# carry those three things is a bug to fix, not a line to add here.
#
# Observed against Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7 (subprojects/capstone.wrap) and
# LLVM 18 MC.  On a Capstone bump: rebuild, re-run
# `python -m champsim_tracer_validator full`, and delete every entry whose
# signature no longer appears.  `capstone_workaround_probe` answers the
# complementary question — whether an existing disas/capstone.c workaround
# has become unnecessary.

# =====================================================================
# 1. Where LLVM is the one that is wrong, or models something that is not
#    architectural.  These are not Capstone defects and never will be
#    "fixed"; they are removable only if LLVM's own descriptions change.
# =====================================================================

# LLVM's MIPS BC1T/BC1F/BC1TL/BC1FL carry `Defs = [AT]`, an ASSEMBLER-MACRO
# artifact: the assembler may expand a long-range FP branch through $at, so
# the pseudo-instruction claims to clobber it.  A branch writes no GPR at
# all.  Capstone inherited the same claim through the TableGen sync, and
# disas/capstone.c now removes it (see the FP condition-code workaround) —
# which is exactly why this shows up as "Capstone is missing a write".
# Keeping the phantom would fabricate a WAW hazard on $at across every FP
# branch.  Removable when LLVM stops modelling the macro expansion in
# MCInstrDesc, which is not expected.
mipsel R-wr-missing bc1* +r#

# `jalr rd, rs` WITH AN EXPLICIT rd.  MIPS32 defines JALR as
# `GPR[rd] <- return_addr; PC <- GPR[rs]` and rd defaults to 31 only when
# the assembly omits it, so `jalr $sp,$v0` links into $sp and leaves $ra
# alone.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc carries `Defs = [RA]` on the JALR opcode
# regardless -- its pattern is written for the one-operand spelling -- and
# Capstone's MIPS tables are GENERATED FROM LLVM, so the two decoders
# agreeing is one source counted twice rather than two references.  Both
# are refuted by the ISA manual and by QEMU at its own decode site:
# gen_compute_branch() sets `blink = rt` and emits exactly one link store,
# measured in the extractor's own dump on 0320c009 as `w reg=t8` with no
# `ra` beside it.  disas/capstone.c now drops the phantom, gated on
# Capstone's own operand list naming a link target other than $ra -- so
# `jalr $t9`, whose rd IS $ra, keeps the write.  Same class and same
# disposition as the bc1* row above: keeping it would fabricate a WAW
# hazard on the return-address register across every indirect call that
# links elsewhere.  Removable when LLVM stops modelling the default-rd
# spelling in the opcode's Defs, which is not expected.
mipsel R-wr-missing jalr +r#

# (The MIPS branch-and-link and JR / JALR target-read entries that used to
# sit here were an artifact of this tool, not a disagreement.  Its call-site
# suppression of LLVM's AArch64 ABI stack-pointer use spelled the non-
# AArch64 register `r2` — the RISC-V stack pointer — and applied it to every
# ISA, so on MIPS it deleted $v0 from the read set of anything LLVM calls a
# call.  `jalr $t0` compared clean and `jalr $v0` did not; signature keys
# collapse register numbers, so one erased value read as a class-wide LLVM
# defect.  The justification that stood here said LLVM "reports no reads at
# all" for these forms.  It never did.)
mipsel B-ret-mismatch jr cs-ret

# (LWL/LWR read their own destination — the partial-merge workaround in
# disas/capstone.c supplies it, LLVM models the merge as a tied operand on
# the *store* side only.  Covered by the explicit `lwl` / `lwr` entries in
# section 3; the class-wide form that used to live here spelled the family
# `lw[lr]`, which the matcher reads literally — `*` is its only wildcard —
# so it never matched anything.)

# LLVM gives every SSE/AVX-class RISC-V FP vector op `Uses = [FRM]` but, in
# LLVM 18, drops the `[VL, VTYPE]` the base class supplies — so the FP
# vector ops claim not to depend on the vector configuration that decides
# how many elements they process.  Architecturally they do, and
# disas/capstone.c supplies the reads (see the RVV vector-configuration
# workaround).  Removable when LLVM's RISCVInstrInfoV* descriptions carry
# VL/VTYPE on the FP forms; re-check by running this sweep against a newer
# llvm-config.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vs*.v +vl,vtype

# The RVV unconditionally-masked carry/merge families (`.vvm` / `.vxm` /
# `.vim` / `.vfm`) take v0 as a mandatory DATA operand: the carry-in of
# vadc/vmadc, the borrow-in of vsbc/vmsbc, the select control of
# vmerge/vfmerge.  RVV v1.0 §11.4, §11.5 and §11.15 all define it as read.
# BOTH decoders print `v0` in the operand string and NEITHER reports the
# read, so this is the one disagreement class this tool could never have
# discovered — it was fixed from the specification (see the v0 restore in
# cap_fill_generic_operands) and carries its own regression check,
# probe_rv_v0_carry_mask, precisely because the sweep cannot cover it.
# Without the read a multiword add chain's carry serialisation vanishes.
# The unmasked siblings (`vmadc.vv`, `vmsbc.vx`) genuinely do not read v0
# and are deliberately not matched.  Removable when LLVM's
# RISCVInstrInfoV descriptions mark the V0 operand a use.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vadc.v*m +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmadc.v*m +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vsbc.v*m +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsbc.v*m +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmerge.v*m +v#
# vfmerge is vector FP, so its signature also carries the frm read the
# unit always makes (R7.4, cap_riscv_reads_dynamic_frm); re-pointed
# rather than deleted, because the v0 justification above is unchanged.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vfmerge.v*m +frm,v#

# (`aarch64 R-rd-missing bl* +sp` used to sit here, over LLVM's ABI-
# modelling implicit SP use on AArch64 calls.  The suppression it said was
# already working ran BEFORE the loop that inserts the implicit uses, so it
# suppressed nothing at all; the glob then hid every form, plain and
# pointer-authenticated alike.  The suppression happens where the use
# enters now, and none of them disagree.)

# LLVM gives AArch64 MRS/MSR an NZCV def that the architecture does not —
# for MOST members.  The def is attached to the whole
# MSR-immediate-to-PSTATE-field form, because one of the fields it can name
# IS NZCV — so every alias of that form inherits it, including the SME
# streaming-mode switches, which write PSTATE.SM and PSTATE.ZA and leave
# the condition flags alone.
#
# ONE alias under the `mrs` key is the other way around, and this comment
# used to paper over it: `mrs xN, RNDR` / `RNDRRS` really does set NZCV
# (Z reports entropy failure), LLVM is RIGHT to def it there, and Capstone
# reports no flags write — a real Capstone gap riding the same collapsed
# signature as the phantoms (verified --hex=02243bd5, 2026-08-12: llvm
# WR{nzcv,r2}, boundary WR{r2}).  Work item filed; the fields-layer twin in
# isaxcheck_fields_allow.txt carries the same carve-out.  Weight today is
# nil (no traced workload reads RNDR), which is why carrying it is
# tolerable, not why it is right.
aarch64 R-wr-missing mrs +nzcv
aarch64 R-wr-missing msr +nzcv
aarch64 R-wr-missing smstart +nzcv
aarch64 R-wr-missing smstop +nzcv

# MSR (immediate) -- the PSTATE fields, and SMSTART / SMSTOP.
#
# The boundary translates the SYSALIAS pstatefield operand into the system
# register the field IS -- DAIF, SPSel, PAN, UAO, DIT, SSBS, TCO, ALLINT,
# PM, SVCR -- so `msr daifset, #2` and `msr daif, x0` are one dependency
# node instead of two unrelated ones, and derives the same answer a second
# time from the encoding (cap_aarch64_msr_imm_contract).  LLVM MC models
# none of it: for the whole family its Defs are `nzcv` (allowlisted three
# lines up, and wrong -- MSR (immediate) writes no condition flag) and its
# Uses are empty.  So every one of these is the tracer recording state LLVM
# does not, which is the direction that is never a defect.
#
# Direction comes from msr_imm.xml's `case field of`, not from convention:
# DAIFSet/DAIFClr are `PSTATE.D = PSTATE.D OR operand<3>` and friends, a
# read-modify-write, hence +sys on BOTH sides; SPSel/PAN/UAO/DIT/TCO/SSBS/
# ALLINT/PM write a one-bit field whole, hence a write only.  The SVCR
# forms read SVCR too -- SetPSTATE_SM calls ResetSVEState() only when the
# mode CHANGES -- and carry the SME enable gate, because msr_imm.xml calls
# CheckSMEAccess() and R7.4 makes that read a source.  Capstone attaches
# this encoding no feature group at all, so the gate cannot come from
# cap_aarch64_is_fp_gated and comes from the encoding instead.
#
# These four lines REPLACE `aarch64 Z-unmodelled-op msr` and
# `aarch64 Z-unmodelled-op smstart`, which the tool now reports DEAD: the
# operand they excused as unmodelled is modelled.
aarch64 R-rd-phantom msr +sys
aarch64 R-rd-phantom smstart +sysfpen,vctrl
aarch64 R-rd-phantom smstop +sysfpen,vctrl
aarch64 R-wr-phantom smstart +vctrl
aarch64 R-wr-phantom smstop +vctrl

# The TLBI operations that take NO Xt argument (TLBI VMALLE1, VMALLS12E1IS,
# ALLE2, …) are still encoded in the SYS format, which has an Rt field; the
# architecture requires that field to be 0b11111 for them.  LLVM keeps the
# syntactic operand and reports it read whatever it holds, so an encoding
# that puts a real register number in an Rt the operation does not use looks
# like a lost read.  Capstone is right — the operation reads no register —
# and the disagreement exists only on reserved encodings no assembler emits
# (`tlbi vmalls12e1is` written properly names XZR, which folds away).  The
# argument-taking forms (`tlbi vae2is, x0`) agree on both sides.  Newly
# reachable since the sweep started filling Rn=30 (see sweep_aarch64).
aarch64 R-rd-missing tlbi +r#

# CWD / CDQ / CQO sign-extend the accumulator into the D register and leave
# the accumulator alone.  LLVM's implicit-def list names it anyway --
# llvm_opcode_table.tsv has `OPC CDQ ... IDEF:EAX,EDX, IUSE:EAX,` and the
# same for CQO -- which is the register-allocator convenience LLVM's x86
# implicit lists are known for, not an architectural claim.
#
# The architecture is explicit.  Intel SDM Vol. 2, CWD/CDQ/CQO "Operation":
#   IF OperandSize = 16 THEN DX := SignExtend(AX);
#   ELSE IF OperandSize = 32 EDX := SignExtend(EAX);
#   ELSE IF 64-Bit Mode and OperandSize = 64 RDX := SignExtend(RAX);
# with no assignment to the source and "Flags Affected: None".  XED agrees
# operand for operand -- `xed -64 -v 5 -d 99` gives REG0/W/EDX and
# REG1/R/EAX -- and so does iced-x86.
#
# The boundary drops Capstone's matching phantom (cap_x86_is_sign_extend_to_d)
# because these sit immediately before a signed IDIV: an rax marked written
# makes the trace name the CQO as the dividend's producer and hides the real
# one.  So the tracer is short a write LLVM reports, deliberately.
#
# Delete these three if LLVM ever narrows CDQ/CQO/CWD to Defs = [xDX].
#
# THE IMPLICIT-REGISTER TABLE (disas/capstone.c, cap_x86_implicit) MOVED
# THIRTEEN RULES IN THIS FILE FROM LIVE TO DEAD, AND THEY ARE DELETED
# RATHER THAN KEPT.  Every one of them said "the tracer misses a register
# LLVM names" for an instruction whose whole dependency is implicit --
# xlatb, cmpxchg8b/16b, clzero, monitorx, mwaitx.  Capstone reports no
# implicit operand for them; the table supplies the architectural set, so
# the signature cannot occur any more and a rule that cannot fire is a
# false statement about the tracer.
#
# THE ROWS BELOW ARE WHAT THE REPAIR LEFT, in three classes, and none of
# them is a register the tracer dropped:
#
#  *-phantom  fs / gs / cr0 on SMSW, LMSW, SWAPGS, RD/WRFSBASE,
#             RD/WRGSBASE, LFS, LGS: the tracer names the control or
#             segment register and LLVM MC names NOTHING.  XED -- this
#             arc's primary reference -- names exactly what the tracer
#             now names, on the same encodings:
#               0f01e0    SMSW      XED src CR0        LLVM src -
#               0f01f0    LMSW      XED dst CR0        LLVM dst -
#               0f01f8    SWAPGS    XED GSBASE/GSBASE  LLVM -/-
#               f30faec0  RDFSBASE  XED src FSBASE     LLVM src -
#               f30faed0  WRFSBASE  XED dst FSBASE     LLVM dst -
#               0fb400    LFS       XED dst FS,RAX     LLVM dst RAX
#             LLVM MC models these as opaque; QEMU's translation of each
#             reads or writes precisely that state.  Recording MORE than
#             LLVM is the direction this project accepts.
#
#  mwaitx     the tracer writes RAX where LLVM writes nothing.  XED
#             writes RAX (the MONITORX/MWAITX extension returns its
#             status there).  The RBX read the tracer also adds is NOT
#             in this list because it made the tracer AGREE with LLVM.
#
#  *-missing  cs / ds on cmpxchg8b, cmpxchg16b and ud1 with a segment
#             override.  This is the SAME pre-existing class already
#             allowed here for cmpxchgb, cmpw and the rest -- the tracer
#             does not attribute the segment register of an explicit
#             memory operand -- and these three mnemonics only join it
#             now because the register half of their disagreement was
#             repaired and stopped masking the segment half.  The rule
#             they replace said "+REG_GPR#,REG_SEG0"; the narrower rule
#             is the honest one.
#
#             MONITORX / MWAITX with a 66, F2 or F3 prefix are the two
#             rows where the tracer now records LESS than LLVM MC, and
#             that is deliberate: XED REFUSES every prefixed form of both
#             (660f01fa, 660f01fb, f20f01fa, f20f01fb, f30f01fb all
#             decode to nothing) and reads f30f01fa as MCOMMIT, while
#             LLVM ignores the prefix and repeats the bare instruction.
#             The implicit-register table follows XED and declines to
#             attach MONITORX's operands to bytes XED says are not
#             MONITORX.
#
#  ud1        Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7 decoded UD1 (0F B9 /r) as two bytes
#             with no operands, which the same repair fixes; the row now
#             enters the comparison at all, and LLVM's own view of it
#             carries a memory operand for the register form and no
#             control-flow group.  Both differences are LLVM's.
#
x86_64 R-wr-missing cltd +r#
x86_64 R-wr-missing cqto +r#
x86_64 R-wr-missing cwtd +r#

# =====================================================================
# 2. Deliberate tracer modelling decisions.  The disagreement is real but
#    the tracer's answer is the intended one, so "fixing" it would be a
#    regression.  Removable only if the modelling decision changes.
# =====================================================================

# Memory-hint and cache-maintenance instructions (AArch64 PRF*, MIPS
# CACHE/CACHEE/SYNCI) emit no TCG memop.  The tracer classifies them as
# GEN_OP_PREFETCH / GEN_OP_CACHE_FLUSH and synthesises the address slot from
# the opcode class, so the boundary leaves their MEM operand's access bits
# empty — see cap_aarch64_infer_mem_access(), which returns 0 for the
# prefetch classes on purpose, and the GEN_OP_PREFETCH comment in
# champsim_tracer_generic_ids.h.  LLVM reports mayLoad.
#
# `mipsel Z-access0 pref* MEM` USED TO BE HERE AND ITS JUSTIFICATION WAS
# FALSE.  The sentence it rested on -- "rather than minting a load lane that
# no memory callback will ever fill" -- was measured and is not true of
# `pref`: the tracer synthesises the effective address and the record
# reaches the wire, so with no lane declared it arrived ORPHANED, rendering
# `ld(0x410180)` in parentheses instead of `ld[%gp5](0x410180)`.  x86_64
# `prefetcht1` and aarch64 `prfm` declare loads=1 and always did.  The rule
# is gone because the access is no longer 0, and the dead-rule detector is
# what said so.
#
# `synci`'S DIRECTION IS NOW ANSWERED, and the answer is that there is no
# store.  QEMU lowers MIPS SYNCI to a bare `ctx->base.is_jmp = DISAS_STOP`
# with no TCG memory op and no address computation, in every regime
# (target/mips/tcg/translate.c:14403, micromips_translate.c.inc:2420 and
# :2456, nanomips_translate.c.inc:3991); LLVM MC reports mayLoad=0
# mayStore=0; and binutils, the one reference that says STORE, gives the
# same construct three different answers (`pref` LM, `cache` neither,
# `synci` SM).  The synthesised record stays load-shaped -- an address with
# data_size 0, which is what `prfm` and `pref` publish -- because the wire
# has two directions and this needs a third.  Adjudicated in
# arc3_cov/mipsel/memop_presence.py as `cache-maintenance-direction`.
#
# THE SAME TREATMENT NOW REACHES AArch64 CACHE MAINTENANCE BY VIRTUAL
# ADDRESS.  `dc cvau, Xt` / `ic ivau, Xt` name an effective address and act
# on the line containing it; Capstone models no memory operand for the whole
# DC/IC alias space, so the boundary mints one from Xt with EMPTY access bits
# (cap_aarch64_is_va_cache_maint_sysop in disas/capstone.c).  Empty is the
# literal truth -- QEMU implements them as ARM_CP_NOP -- and it is what the
# synthetic-EA class keys on.  LLVM reports no memory operand at all, hence
# the M-cs-mem-llvm-none row for `ic`; `dc` already has one, for DC ZVA.
aarch64 Z-access0 prf* MEM
aarch64 M-miss-load prf*
aarch64 Z-access0 dc MEM
aarch64 Z-access0 ic MEM
aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none ic
mipsel Z-access0 cache* MEM
mipsel Z-access0 synci MEM
mipsel M-cs-mem-llvm-none pref*
mipsel M-cs-mem-llvm-none cache*
mipsel M-cs-mem-llvm-none synci

# SVE vector ADR computes an address without accessing memory — the AArch64
# equivalent of x86 LEA.  An access-free MEM operand is the correct
# representation and the operand walker skips it for exactly that reason
# ("LEA / prefetch-hint MEM operands lack both READ and WRITE — no real
# memop, don't count", champsim_tracer_decode.cc).
aarch64 Z-access0 adr MEM
aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none adr

# =====================================================================
# 3. Feature sets the tracer does not model.  These are honest gaps with a
#    named boundary, documented in docs/limitations.rst.
# =====================================================================

# SME.  The ZA matrix accumulator and its tile/slice operands arrive as
# Capstone AARCH64_OP_SME operands, for which the plugin operand ABI has no
# representation (there is one register slot per operand and an SME operand
# names a tile, a slice-select GPR and an immediate index).  Every SME
# instruction therefore loses its accumulator dependency.  The tracer does
# not target SME workloads and QEMU's SME support is itself partial.
# Closing this needs an operand-ABI extension, not a boundary fix.
# (was a class-wide rule; narrowed to explicit signatures so a NEW
#  unmodelled operand type on AArch64 still fails the gate)
#  the complementary `R-*-phantom * +za*` form is deliberately absent: the
#  boundary drops SME operands, so a ZA register can only ever be MISSING
#  from its sets, never phantom in them.)

# SVE2.1 / SME2 predicate-as-counter PAIR destinations: `whilege
# { p14.b, p15.b }, ...` writes two predicate registers and Capstone reports
# only the first.  Single-predicate WHILE* forms — which is what any SVE
# (as opposed to SME2) workload emits — are correct, and are verified by the
# predicate end-to-end test.  Re-check on a Capstone bump with
# `cstool -d arm64 ff5f2025`.
aarch64 R-wr-missing while* +p#
aarch64 R-wr-missing p*ext* +p#
aarch64 R-wr-missing pext +p#

# RISC-V CSR operands.  Capstone types the CSR-number operand as one the
# boundary cannot map to a register, so a CSR access shows no CSR
# dependency.  The tracer models CSRs only through the specific ones the ISA
# tables name (frm/fflags -> REG_FCSR, vl/vtype -> REG_VCTRL); a general CSR
# index has no generic register id.  Documented in docs/limitations.rst.

# (The two `c*cmsa` entries that used to stand here were not a disagreement
# between the decoders at all.  They said the bank distinction between the
# MSA control registers was inexpressible, but what the tool was actually
# reporting is that it could not tell Capstone's bare control-register
# NUMBER and LLVM's MSA control-register NAME were the same operand.  The
# MIPS normaliser folds LLVM's spelling onto the number now, using the
# register-number column of the MSA specification, and CFCMSA agrees
# outright.  What survives is one real difference, on CTCMSA only, and it
# is listed at the end of this file with the reason it happens.  The FP
# control registers keep their own entries below; only the MSA half of the
# old block was a spelling artifact.)
# (The ten `*c2` coprocessor-register entries that used to open this block
# were an artifact of this tool, not a disagreement.  Its MIPS normaliser
# dropped the coprocessor index by keeping only the LAST digit, so COP2
# register 15 -- LLVM's `COP215` -- read as `cop5` and compared against the
# wrong register for every coprocessor register above 9.  The index comes
# off the front now.  The blind spot that hid it is the same one the sweep
# fill sets exist to close: Rd never took a value large enough to reach a
# two-digit coprocessor register until (31, 0) was added to the MIPS fills.)
mipsel R-rd-missing cfc1 +fcsr
mipsel R-rd-phantom cfc1 +cop#
mipsel R-wr-missing ctc1 +fcsr
mipsel R-wr-phantom ctc1 +cop#

# (MIPS DSP accumulators $ac1..$ac3 on the two-operand MADD/MSUB forms:
# Capstone names only the implicit HI:LO pair.  The tracer's HI:LO chain is
# intact (probe_implicit_acc pins it); the extra DSP accumulators are not.
# Covered by the explicit madd / maddu / msub / msubu entries in section 3;
# the class-wide form that used to live here spelled the family
# `m[as][du][db]*`, which the matcher reads literally and never matched.)

# =====================================================================
# 4. The subtarget gap (class D-cs-only): the boundary decoded it and
#    LLVM did not.
#
#    THIS CLASS IS SPECIAL AND THE TOOL ENFORCES IT.  A `D-cs-only`
#    signature is not one finding among many — it is an encoding on which
#    the register, memory and branch comparisons DO NOT RUN AT ALL, because
#    a rejected LLVM decode short-circuits the compare.  The number of
#    signatures here is therefore the size of this gate's blind spot, and
#    isaxcheck reports it as `subtarget_gap=<signatures>/<encodings>` in
#    its summary line.
#
#    Two rules follow, and isaxcheck refuses an allowlist that breaks the
#    first of them:
#
#      * NO WILDCARDS.  `aarch64 D-cs-only *` was one line, and it hid
#        every extension family a Capstone bump might add.  Each signature
#        is named, or the entry is refused at load time.
#      * A LINE HERE IS THE SECOND-CHOICE FIX.  The first choice is to add
#        the missing feature to kIsaTable so the gap CLOSES.  That is what
#        happened to mipsel (+mips3d) and to the whole of riscv64, which
#        now reports subtarget_gap=0/0 — and closing the RISC-V gap is what
#        made the Zacas `amocas.*` dropped destination visible at all.
#
#    So the only entries below are AArch64's, where `+all` is already the
#    widest subtarget LLVM has and the residue is Capstone accepting
#    encodings the architecture does not define.  See also
#    docs/limitations.rst, "Wrong-path decode of arbitrary bytes".
# =====================================================================

# (a) Architecturally-fixed fields holding reserved values.  `0x88c0e022`
# has Rs != 0b11111 and is therefore not a valid LDAR, but Capstone decodes
# it as `ldar w2, [x1]` while LLVM rejects it.  The same shape covers the
# load/store-exclusive and acquire/release families (Rs and Rt2 must both
# read 0b11111), ADDG/SUBG (op3 must be 0b00) and SB (CRm must be 0b0000).
# On the correct path the bytes never occur.  On the wrong path, where the
# tracer decodes arbitrary bytes, it means a template can describe a
# plausible instruction where the hardware would raise UNDEFINED; the
# tracer's wrong-path fidelity contract already treats wrong-path
# instruction identity as best-effort.
# AXFLAG and XAFLAG join on the same terms from the other side of the
# encoding space: they are MSR-immediate forms whose CRm field the
# architecture fixes at 0b0000, and `0xd500415f` sets CRm = 0b0001.  The
# properly-encoded `0xd500405f` decodes identically on both sides, which is
# how the two were told apart.
# Verify with `cstool -d arm64 22e0c088` on a Capstone bump: if Capstone
# starts rejecting the reserved encodings, every line here goes dead and
# the tool says so.
aarch64 D-cs-only addg
aarch64 D-cs-only axflag
aarch64 D-cs-only ldar

# (b) CONSTRAINED UNPREDICTABLE register aliasing on the pair forms.  The
# architecture leaves `ldp w2, w2, [x1], #28` (Rt == Rt2) and
# `stp wzr, w0, [x0], #40` (writeback Rn == Rt2) undefined; LLVM refuses
# them, Capstone decodes them.  This is the same best-effort wrong-path
# space as (a) — the difference is that the fields are legal in isolation
# and only their COMBINATION is not, so no field check would catch it.
aarch64 D-cs-only ldaxp
aarch64 D-cs-only ldnp
aarch64 D-cs-only ldp
aarch64 D-cs-only ldpsw
aarch64 D-cs-only ldxp
aarch64 D-cs-only stgp
aarch64 D-cs-only stp

# The single-register half of the same family, reachable since the sweep
# started filling Rt == Rn (see sweep_aarch64).  A writeback load whose
# transfer register IS its base register — `ldr w0, [x0], #1`,
# `ldr w0, [x0, #0]!`, and the pointer-authenticated `ldraa`/`ldrab` — has
# two definitions of one register and the architecture leaves it
# CONSTRAINED UNPREDICTABLE.  LLVM SoftFails, Capstone decodes; the store
# forms are unconstrained and agree, which is how the boundary of the
# family was located.
aarch64 D-cs-only ldr
aarch64 D-cs-only ldraa
aarch64 D-cs-only ldrab
aarch64 D-cs-only ldrb
aarch64 D-cs-only ldrh
aarch64 D-cs-only ldrsb
aarch64 D-cs-only ldrsh

# (c) THE ONE BELOW IS AN LLVM DEFECT, not a subtarget gap, and it is
# recorded here only because a rejected LLVM decode is what the class
# means.  `ldtr w0, [x0, #1]` is a perfectly well-defined instruction:
# LDTR is the UNPRIVILEGED load, it has no writeback, and the ARM ARM puts
# no constraint on Rt == Rn for it.  LLVM refuses it anyway, because
# AArch64Disassembler.cpp's DecodeSignedLdStInstruction() tests
#
#     bool IsIndexed = fieldFromInstruction(insn, 10, 2) != 0;
#     // Cannot write back to a transfer register (but xzr != sp).
#     if (IsLoad && IsIndexed && !IsFP && Rn != 31 && Rt == Rn)
#       return SoftFail;
#
# and bits [11:10] select the addressing form: 0b01 post-index, 0b11
# pre-index — and 0b10 LDTR, which writes back nothing.  The check's own
# comment says what it is for; the predicate admits one form it was not
# meant to cover.  Fix upstream: exclude 0b10 from IsIndexed.  Capstone
# and the tracer are right, so nothing here needs changing, and these
# lines go dead when LLVM is fixed.
aarch64 D-cs-only ldtr
aarch64 D-cs-only ldtrb
aarch64 D-cs-only ldtrh
aarch64 D-cs-only ldtrsb
aarch64 D-cs-only ldtrsh

# =====================================================================
# 4b. Residual class-R/Z findings on the RISC-V space the closed subtarget
#     gap made visible.  These were inside "LLVM rejects this" until the
#     kIsaTable feature list was completed; none of them is reachable by a
#     binary the tracer traces today, and each names its fix.
# =====================================================================

# RVV whole-register MOVE reports the vector-context CSRs it does not read.
# `vmv2r.v` copies a register GROUP by architecture, independent of vl and
# vtype -- that is the point of the instruction -- and Capstone attaches the
# RVV context reads anyway.  Same family as the vl/vtype phantoms above, and
# reached only once (1, 0) joined the RISC-V sweep fills: the whole-register
# moves need rd = v0 to print their two-operand form.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmv2r.v +vl,vtype

riscv64 R-rd-missing *ntl* +r#

# RISC-V hint encodings whose architectural effect is "nothing":
# `c.addi rd, 0`, `c.slli64`, `c.srai64`, `c.srli64` are HINTs.  Capstone
# reports no write (correct for a hint); LLVM reports the nominal write.
# It reports the nominal READ too, and that half the tracer does not keep:
# the dependency model drops it so a HINT arrives as a whole no-op rather
# than as a source with no destination.  That repair is one layer down and
# asserted in isaxcheck_fixups.txt (`riscv64 W-rd-dropped c.addi -*`), so
# the read still agrees HERE — this entry covers only the write.
riscv64 R-wr-missing c.* +r#

# RISC-V JAL/JALR link-register write: the alias-hidden link dataflow is
# recovered inside the plugin by probe_rv_link_dataflow (verified
# end-to-end: `call $0x105cc -> %lr`), which is downstream of the boundary
# this tool measures.  The trace is correct; the boundary alone is not.
riscv64 R-wr-missing jal +r#
riscv64 R-wr-missing jalr +r#
# (`riscv64 R-rd-phantom jalr +r#` used to close this block.  It was the
# same tool artifact the MIPS note in section 1 describes, and on RISC-V the
# erased token `r2` IS the stack pointer: `jalr ra, 0(sp)` lost the one
# register it reads.  LLVM reported it correctly throughout.)

# =====================================================================
# 5. Explicit residual baseline.
#
# Everything below is one line per observed signature, not a wildcard: a
# NEW disagreement still fails the gate.  These are the signatures that
# survive the fixes and do not fall into a family above.  They have been
# swept into three buckets by inspection, and the bucket is named in the
# comment that precedes each block; what they have in common is that none
# of them is a lost or fabricated dependency on an instruction a compiler
# emits for the ISA subsets the tracer targets.
#
# To regenerate after a Capstone or LLVM bump:
#   build/contrib/plugins/isaxcheck --isa=<isa> --jobs=8 [--mattr=...] \
#       --allow=<this file> --check
# then move any signature that is genuinely new into the right section
# above, or fix it.  Do not regenerate this block wholesale without
# reading the diff — that is how a real regression gets blessed.
# =====================================================================

# --- aarch64: SIMD structure / SVE gather register-set shape differences,
#     SVE2.1-only forms, and the load/store-multiple writeback tuple naming.
#     Each was inspected: the register is present on both sides under a
#     different tuple spelling, or belongs to a feature above.
# Exception generation.  Capstone puts BRK / HLT / SVC / HVC / SMC in a
# control group and LLVM's MCInstrDesc gives them none — it describes them
# as ordinary instructions with an immediate, because a trap's edge is not
# one a compiler's CFG follows.  The tracer needs the edge: a trap ends a
# true BB.  `svc` and `smc` are here rather than only `hvc` because the
# sweep now fills Rd = 1 and Rd = 3, which is what selects them (see
# sweep_aarch64); the classification they get is asserted in
# isaxcheck_fixups.txt, which is the file that would notice it regressing.
aarch64 B-flow-presence hvc cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 B-flow-presence smc cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 B-flow-presence svc cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none dc
# GCSSTR / GCSSTTR store Xt to the Guarded Control Stack at [Xn].  Capstone
# models no memory operand (both operands come back as plain register reads
# and its printer drops the brackets), so the boundary mints the written MEM
# operand from Xn -- see the GCSSTR block in cap_fill_arm64_operands.  LLVM's
# MCInstrDesc names no memory operand either; the Arm MRA's execute-ASL
# resolves the row to mw=1, which is the side this follows.
aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none gcsstr
aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none gcssttr
aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none ld64b

aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none st64bv0
aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none stgm
aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none stzgm
# mayLoad / mayStore are LLVM SCHEDULING properties, not semantics: they
# say "do not move memory across me", which is precisely what a barrier is
# for, and LLVM sets both on the whole barrier and hint space to get that
# ordering for free.  None of these instructions touches memory, and
# Capstone reports no memory operand for any of them.  `bti`, `hint`, `nop`
# and `tcancel` were the members the old fills happened to reach; sweeping
# the hint selector (see sweep_aarch64) reaches the rest of the space, and
# it is the same artifact throughout.  `setffr` / `wrffr` are the SVE
# first-fault register writers, where the mayStore is LLVM modelling FFR as
# ordered state rather than as a register.
aarch64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none bti load store
aarch64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none clrbhb load store
aarch64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none clrex load store

aarch64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none mov load store

aarch64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none psb load store
aarch64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none pssbb load store

aarch64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none sev load store

# THE AArch64 LOAD-EXCLUSIVES, and the reason they only became visible at
# 733a65dfb0.  LLVM sets mayStore on ldxr / ldaxr / ldxp / ldaxp (with the
# byte and halfword forms) the same way it sets it on the barriers above:
# the exclusive monitor is unmodelled state, and mayStore is how the
# scheduler is told not to move memory across it.  Architecturally a
# load-exclusive reads memory and marks the monitor; it writes nothing.
#
# Until 733a65dfb0 nothing appeared here, and that was two errors
# cancelling rather than agreement: Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7 reports
# READ|WRITE on the MEM operand of these forms, so the boundary claimed a
# store too and the comparison matched.  The Capstone side is now
# corrected (cap_aarch64_exclusive_mem_access), and the disagreement it
# leaves is LLVM's alone.
#
# Which side is right was MEASURED, not argued: on an aarch64 probe the
# ldaxr entry declared `st[%gp1]` with no address and every execution
# delivered only `ld=`, never `st=` -- a slot QEMU's memop callbacks can
# never fill.  ldar is the control (READ only, unchanged) and the LSE
# read-modify-writes (ldadd, swp) keep both slots, so this is specific to
# the exclusives rather than a blanket rule about acquire/release.
# Evidence: /mnt/md0/QEMU/cst_runs/p3/t177/FINDING.md.
aarch64 M-miss-store ldaxp
aarch64 M-miss-store ldaxr
aarch64 M-miss-store ldaxrb
aarch64 M-miss-store ldaxrh
aarch64 M-miss-store ldxp
aarch64 M-miss-store ldxr
aarch64 M-miss-store ldxrb
aarch64 M-miss-store ldxrh

aarch64 M-miss-store prfb
aarch64 M-miss-store prfd
aarch64 M-miss-store prfh
aarch64 M-miss-store prfw
aarch64 R-wr-missing pext +p#
aarch64 R-wr-missing whilegt +p#
aarch64 R-wr-missing whilehi +p#
aarch64 R-wr-missing whilele +p#
aarch64 R-wr-missing whilels +p#
aarch64 R-wr-phantom ldg +r#
aarch64 R-wr-phantom ldg +sp

# --- x86_64 baseline.
#
# x86 is swept for REGRESSION detection only, and its residual is recorded
# here wholesale rather than triaged signature by signature.  The reason is
# that x86 already has an independent, DYNAMIC ground truth that this static
# check cannot match: the Intel PIN pairing, which found the five defects the
# existing disas/capstone.c x86 workarounds address and which remains the
# authority for effective addresses, real access sizes, rep fan-out and
# segment interactions.  Against LLVM MC the x86 residual is dominated by
# systematic modelling differences that describe no defect — `bnd` versus
# `repne` prefix printing, LLVM's shadow-stack and flag-modelling
# artifacts, and AT&T operand-order effects.  A fourth used to be listed
# here, prefix-consumption disagreements (Capstone folds `64 65` into one
# instruction, LLVM stops at `gs`); those encodings are no longer scored
# at all — see the size_gap paragraph at the end of this comment.  A handful of entries here ARE candidate defects worth their own
# arc (`adox` losing its destination read, `bswapw`, `cmpxchg8b`/`16b`
# losing mayStore, `cldemote`); they are tracked as x86 work, not as part of
# the non-x86 decode-metadata arc this gate was built for.
#
# "RECORDED WHOLESALE" IS WHAT MAKES THIS BLOCK DANGEROUS, and two of its
# rows have already had to be pulled back out of it.  `M-extra-load` and
# `M-miss-store` on fisttpl, fisttpll and fisttps sat here as residual and
# were a Capstone access-flag defect -- the truncating x87 integer stores
# reported as loads -- now repaired at the boundary
# (cap_x86_x87_mem_access in disas/capstone.c) and deleted.  A wholesale
# block cannot be read as evidence that its members were examined; the
# only rows in it that HAVE been examined are the ones this comment names.
#
# `M-extra-load movq` and `M-miss-store movq` stay, with the justification
# they actually have.  Their sample is `f3480f7e00`, where Capstone says
# `movq (%rax), %xmm0` and LLVM says `movq %mm0, (%rax)`: the two decoders
# have decoded DIFFERENT INSTRUCTIONS from one byte string.  F3 0F 7E is
# MOVQ xmm1, xmm2/m64 and REX.W is ignored on it; LLVM lets the REX.W pick
# the MMX form 0F 7E and drops the mandatory F3.  GNU binutils reads it
# Capstone's way (`rex.W movq (%rax),%xmm0`), so it is two-to-one against
# LLVM and there is nothing at this boundary to repair.  Not the same
# thing as the x87 rows that used to sit beside them, despite the
# identical signature shape.
#
# THE BLOCK LOST 1,668 ROWS AT #169, and the two reasons are not the same
# reason.  1,501 of them named an encoding the comparison NO LONGER RUNS:
# where the two decoders consume a different number of bytes they have
# decoded different instructions, and scoring a five-byte `xrelease xchg`
# against LLVM's one-byte `xrelease` produced a register difference that
# only the length gap created.  isaxcheck now stops before the M/B/R
# classes there and counts the encodings instead (`size_gap=` in the
# summary line; class D still reports the length disagreement itself, and
# gates it here).  So the residual did not get better by 1,501 rows -- the
# question those rows answered was withdrawn, and `size_gap=358/6430995`
# is the standing statement of how much of the sweep that is.
#
# The other 167 rows DID stop disagreeing: `cap_x86_is_cmov` gave CMOVcc
# back the destination read Capstone reports it without (104 rows) and
# corrected FCMOVcc's exchanged operand roles (24), `%riz` / `%eiz` are
# now dropped as the not-a-register LLVM index pseudo they are (105 rows
# across both layers), and `st(1)` / `st1` are one token so the x87
# register operands are compared rather than passed over (33).
#
# What this block still buys: a Capstone bump that changes ANY x86 signature
# shows up as a gate failure rather than as silent trace corruption.
x86_64 B-flow-presence iretl cs-no llvm-yes
x86_64 B-flow-presence iretq cs-no llvm-yes
x86_64 B-flow-presence iretw cs-no llvm-yes
x86_64 B-flow-presence loop cs-no llvm-yes
x86_64 B-flow-presence loope cs-no llvm-yes
x86_64 B-flow-presence loopne cs-no llvm-yes

# The x86-64 decode-gap repair (disas/capstone.c, cap_x86_gap_plan) gives the
# boundary a decode for seven families of QEMU-executable bytes Capstone
# 6.0.0-Alpha7 refuses outright.  LLVM MC refuses the same bytes, so every
# repaired family arrives here as D-cs-only: the boundary now describes an
# instruction the reference decoder cannot score.  That is the intended
# trade -- an encoding the trace previously carried with NO opcode, NO
# register set and NO memop is worth more described-but-unscored than
# undescribed -- and each family is scored against Intel XED instead, in
# contrib/plugins/champsim_tracer/tools/arc3_cov/x86_64.
#
# To check whether a line can go: re-run the boundary sweep after a Capstone
# bump.  A family upstream has learned to decode stops reaching the repair,
# and LLVM's verdict on it does not change, so the signature disappears from
# this class and the DEAD-rule detector names the line.
#   fcom / fcomp / fstp / fxch  the reserved x87 alias escapes
#   nopq                        0F 18 /0../3 and 0F 1A..1E, register form
#   prefetch                    0F 0D /3../7, the reserved 3DNow! hints
#   salc                        D6, which QEMU models and XED calls an
#                               undefined byte

x86_64 D-size-mismatch addb
# `f0 d6` -- LLVM decodes the LOCK prefix as a one-byte `lock` instruction
# and stops; the boundary reads it as a prefix and reports the two-byte
# SALC behind it.  Neither length describes something a guest executes:
# LOCK on a non-lockable opcode raises #UD.  Same shape as the xacquire /
# xrelease rows further down.

# FFREE(P)'s st(i) is a tag-word selector, neither read nor written as
# data; the boundary drops the operand outright (cap_x86_is_x87_tag_only,
# disas/capstone.c), which the checker classes as an unmodelled operand.

x86_64 R-wr-missing fxrstor64 +fpsw

x86_64 R-wr-missing movq +r#
x86_64 R-wr-missing wait +fpsw
# The flag WRITE these five perform is real and LLVM MC omits it.  The
# boundary now derives it from Capstone's own detail->x86.eflags bitmask,
# which carries MODIFY_ZF for LAR/LSL/VERR/VERW and the full word for RSM
# (disas/capstone.c, cap_x86_eflags_from_bitmask).  QEMU agrees on every
# one: gen_LAR() movconds the writeback on cpu_cc_src & CC_Z, helper_verr()
# computes `cpu_cc_compute_all(env) | CC_Z`, and RSM's helper restores the
# whole word through cpu_load_eflags().  Intel XED names the write too; the
# LLVM MC leg is the outlier, so this is a REFERENCE gap, not a repair to
# undo.  Deletable when LLVM MC gains the implicit def.
x86_64 R-wr-phantom larl +flags

# --- aarch64: SME operands (ZA tiles and slices) and system-register /
#     system-alias operands, which the plugin operand ABI has no
#     representation for.  Listed explicitly rather than as a class-wide
#     rule so a NEW unmodelled operand type still fails the gate.
#
#     The hint and barrier members below arrived with the hint-selector
#     sweep.  Their unmodelled operand is the option field — DMB/DSB's
#     domain-and-direction (`ish`, `oshld`), ISB's, BTI's target class,
#     PSB/TSB's `csync`, IC's cache-maintenance alias — an operand that
#     names WHICH variant of an ordering or maintenance operation this is.
#     None of them carries a value between instructions, which is what the
#     dependency model records, so there is nothing for the operand ABI to
#     represent; the ordering itself is the consumer's memory model to
#     enforce, from the opcode.
aarch64 Z-unmodelled-op at

# (The three `M-cs-mem-llvm-none prefetch.r/w/i` entries that used to close
# this file recorded LLVM decoding the Zicbop hints as a plain `ori` with no
# memory operand.  `+zicbop` in kIsaTable makes LLVM model them as the
# memory hints they are and the disagreement is gone; the tool's dead-rule
# report is what said so.)

# =====================================================================
# 6. The x86 group-opcode and x87 space, reached once ModRM.reg was swept.
#
#    ModRM.reg is not a register on the group opcodes -- 0x80/0x81/0x83,
#    0xC0/0xC1/0xD0-0xD3, 0xF6/0xF7, 0xFE/0xFF and most of the 0x0F groups
#    -- it is an OPCODE EXTENSION.  Two fixed ModRM tails reached two of its
#    eight values, so six members of every group opcode, and the whole x87
#    escape space (0xD8-0xDF, where reg selects the operation), were outside
#    the swept space by construction.  Sweeping it multiplies the x86
#    population by eight and costs nothing measurable.
#
#    What it found is enumerated below, one line per signature, in six
#    families.  None of them is a lost or fabricated dependency on an
#    instruction a compiler emits for the subset the tracer targets; x86's
#    independent ground truth remains the PIN pairing, and this sweep is
#    regression detection.
# =====================================================================

# (a) The subtarget gap.  Capstone accepts group encodings whose reg field

# CET and cache-maintenance forms the x86-64 LLVM subtarget does not.  Named
# exactly, as every gap signature must be.

# (b) The two decoders cut a prefix-heavy byte string into different
# instructions.  With ModRM.reg swept, the tails reach byte strings where
# LLVM stops after consuming a prefix as a standalone instruction --
# `llvm{rep}`, `llvm{repne}` -- while Capstone consumes the whole thing.
# The register and memory sets then describe two different instructions, so
# the difference is a length disagreement wearing a metadata disagreement's
# clothes.  This is the family that already dominates the x86 residual;
# the wider sweep reaches more of it, not a new kind of it.
#
# THE TEST FOR MEMBERSHIP IS THE SAMPLE ENCODING, NOT THE SHAPE OF THE
# SIGNATURE, and it was applied to every row below one at a time: the tool
# prints `cs{...}  llvm{...}` beside each bucket, and a member of this
# family has an LLVM side that is a bare prefix (`es`, `gs`, `lock`,
# `repne`, `data16`, `rex64`) or `<cs-reject>`.  A row whose two sides
# print the SAME instruction is not in this family whatever it looks like,
# and eleven such rows were standing on this justification.
#
# Ten of them were x87 -- `M-extra-load` and `M-miss-store` on fnstcw,
# fsts, fstps, fstl and fstpl, every one sampled as
# `cs{fnstcw (%rbx, %rcx)} llvm{fnstcw (%rbx,%rcx)}`, the two decoders
# naming one instruction and disagreeing about which way its memory
# operand points.  That was a Capstone access-flag defect, is repaired at
# the boundary now (cap_x86_x87_mem_access in disas/capstone.c), and the
# ten rows are deleted.  The rest are recorded here with what they
# actually are.  They are NOT prefix-length disagreements and must not be
# re-justified as such:
#
#   M-miss-store sarb / sarl / sarw / shlw / shrw  (`d23c0b` =
#     `sarb %cl, (%rbx, %rcx)` on both sides).  Capstone reports the
#     memory operand of the %cl-COUNT shift-to-memory forms (D2 /r,
#     D3 /r) READ-only; they are read-modify-write.  The count-1 (D1) and
#     imm8 (C0 / C1) forms of the same instructions ARE reported
#     READ|WRITE, so this is a defect in specific encodings, not a
#     convention.  OPEN: the tracer records these as pure loads.
#
#   M-miss-store rclb / rcrb  (`c01200` = `rclb $0, (%rdx)` on both
#     sides).  The same lost WRITE, and for the rotates it is EVERY count
#     form -- rol, ror, rcl and rcr to memory come back READ-only at
#     D0/D1/D2/D3 and C0/C1 alike.  OPEN, same consequence.
#
#   R-rd-missing rcrl +flags  (`c11c0700`, one instruction on both
#     sides).  Capstone omits the carry-flag READ that makes RCL / RCR
#     what they are.  Described correctly in section (e) below; this copy
#     of it was mis-filed here.
#
#   M-miss-store pushw  (`66ff3402` = `pushw (%rdx, %rax)` on both
#     sides).  Not a defect: Capstone gives PUSH one MEM operand, its
#     source, and models the stack write through the implicit RSP update,
#     where LLVM's mayStore covers it.  The tracer's GEN_OP_PUSH walker
#     supplies the store -- the fields layer reports loads=1 stores=1 for
#     this encoding -- so nothing is lost.
#
#   M-miss-load lcallq, and with it Z-access0 lcallq MEM, R-wr-phantom
#     lcallq / lcallw / bnd lcalll +r#  (`48ff1c07` =
#     `lcallq *(%rdi, %rax)` on both sides).  Two things, neither about
#     prefixes.  Capstone returns access == 0 on the far pointer an
#     indirect far call READS, so the fields layer records loads=0 for it;
#     and LLVM's far-call descriptions carry no defs at all, which is what
#     the R-wr-phantom rows are -- Capstone naming the RSP write is the
#     correct half.  OPEN (the lost far-pointer load).
x86_64 D-llvm-only aesdecwide256kl
x86_64 D-llvm-only aesencwide256kl

# (c) x87.  The escape opcodes 0xD8-0xDF select their operation through

# =====================================================================
# 7. The control state the boundary started reporting, that LLVM does
#    not describe.
#
#    Three repairs landed after this file was last derived -- the AArch64
#    system-register operand, the RISC-V CSR operand, and the FPCR/NZCV
#    implicit reads -- and they moved the disagreement to the other side.
#    Where the boundary used to drop a register and LLVM named it, the
#    boundary now names state LLVM's MCInstrDesc carries no implicit use
#    for at all.  A `phantom` verdict in this section does NOT mean the
#    tracer invented a dependency: it means the second decoder has no
#    description of that state, the same way it has none for x86 MXCSR
#    (section 1).  Where the two disagree about architectural fact rather
#    than about modelling, the instrument that decides is the
#    implicit-operand table, whose ground truth is the Arm MRA and the
#    Sail model rather than a decoder -- and it now scores these rows OK.
#
#    The system-register TOKEN in these keys is the class the dependency
#    model records (`sys`, `fcsr`, `vctrl`, `vstart`), not the
#    architectural name.  The tracer folds the whole system-register space
#    onto REG_SYS apart from the few it models specifically, so a token per
#    architectural register would key a separate signature for each -- 11445
#    of them on RISC-V, one allowlist line per CSR number.  WHICH system
#    register an instruction touches is a real question and it is the
#    implicit table's, which names each one exactly.
# =====================================================================

# AArch64 FPCR and FPSR, the two halves of the FP control and status
# word, which LLVM MC models neither of.
#
# The boundary states both directions -- cap_aarch64_fp_status_contract()
# in disas/capstone.c, adjudicated against the Arm MRA
# ISA_A64_xml_A_profile-2022-12 execute pseudocode with QEMU's own
# translator as the second reading.  FPCR is the FP datapath's INPUT
# (rounding mode, FZ, DN, FEAT_AFP's AH/NEP/FIZ, FEAT_EBF16's EBF);
# FPSR is its OUTPUT (the cumulative exception bits IXC/IOC/OFC/UFC/IDC
# and the Advanced SIMD saturation bit QC).  Capstone has a register id
# for FPCR and none at all for FPSR, so the read rides the FPCR register
# id while the write is appended as the AARCH64_SYSREG_FPSR system
# register operand -- both reach the same generic register, REG_FCSR, and
# the write reaches FPSR's own CONTENT, which riding the FPCR id did not
# (see cap_aarch64_add_fpsr_write).
#
# `R-rd-phantom ... +fpcr` is the read; `R-wr-phantom ... +fcsr` is the
# write, spelled with the generic name because a system-register operand
# carries no Capstone register name to normalise.  A mnemonic in one
# list and not the other is the contract working: the bit movers (fabs/fneg/fcsel/fdup/fexpa/ftssel in their
# vector and SVE forms) read nothing, and the BF16 dot-product and
# matrix family and the SME array accumulate forms read FPCR without
# writing FPSR.
#
# FMOV IS IN NEITHER LIST, ON ANY FORM.  It sits with FABS and FNEG in
# the bit-mover set but not in their scalar exception: FABS and FNEG go
# through FPCR.NEP's merging door and FMOV does not, which QEMU states
# outright by dispatching the first two through do_fp1_scalar_int_2fn
# (merging=true) and FMOV through do_fp1_scalar_int with merging=FALSE
# (translate-a64.c; commit 64339259a9 gives that as the reason it needed
# the parameter).  The row this replaces was
# `R-rd-phantom fmov +fpcr,sysfpen`, which covered every scalar FMOV
# including FMOV (general) -- `fmov s0, w0`, a bit copy between register
# files that performs no conversion and so has no rounding mode to read.
#
# `R-rd-missing` is the other direction -- state LLVM names and the
# boundary, having adjudicated it away, does not:
#   sqdmulh / sqrdmulh +fpcr  integer saturating arithmetic reads no
#                             control word (sqadd_advsimd.xml); it writes
#                             FPSR.QC, which is the `+fcsr` R-wr-phantom
#                             row
#   eretaa / eretab    +r#    they authenticate ELR_ELx; x30 is untouched
#                             (eretaa.xml)
#   mov / zero         +za*   SME ZERO and unpredicated MOVA write the
#                             array without reading it (zero_za1_ri.xml,
#                             mova_za2_z.xml)
aarch64 R-rd-phantom fabs +fpcr,sysfpen
aarch64 R-rd-phantom fadda +fpcr,sysfpen
aarch64 R-rd-phantom fccmp +fpcr,sysfpen
aarch64 R-rd-phantom fccmpe +fpcr,sysfpen
aarch64 R-rd-phantom fneg +fpcr,sysfpen
aarch64 R-rd-missing eretaa +r#
aarch64 R-rd-missing eretab +r#

# The general system-register space (REG_SYS): AArch64 MRS/MSR/MRRS/MSRR and
# the RISC-V Zicsr accesses.  The boundary reports the register the
# instruction moves; LLVM's description of these instructions has no
# register operand for it at all.
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +sys
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +sys
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +sys
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +sys

# The read-only implementation constants (REG_SYSID) 1b6c4995a5 split out
# of REG_SYS: MIDR_EL1, MPIDR_EL1, REVIDR_EL1, CTR_EL0, DCZID_EL0 and the
# ID_AA64* feature registers.  Same instructions, same structural reason as
# the +sys block above -- the selector moved to a second class, so the
# phantom moved with it.  A read of one of these depends on nothing, which
# is the whole point of the class; what it must not do is share an id with
# writable state.
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +sysid
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +sysid
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +sysid
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +sysid

# The FP control/status register the tracer models specifically (REG_FCSR):
# AArch64 FPSR reached through MSR/MRS, RISC-V fflags / frm / fcsr and the
# FP-alias CSR forms.
#
# FPCR IS A SECOND REGISTER AND NOW HAS A SECOND KEY.  It is the CONTROL
# word, distinct from the accrued-status word FPSR, and it stopped sharing a
# generic id with it when cap_aarch64_sysreg_class began routing it to
# QEMU_PLUGIN_SYSREG_FPCW.  The argument is unchanged and is the block's own:
# LLVM's MRS/MSR descriptions carry the system register as an IMMEDIATE, so
# whichever register the tuple selects is a phantom to the reference.
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +fcsr
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +fcsr
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +fcsr
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +fcsr
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +fpcw
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +fpcw
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +fpcw
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +fpcw

# The other two system registers the tracer models specifically, reached by
# the same MRS/MSR pair once the sweep covered the sysreg selector in [9:5]
# (see sweep_aarch64).  LLVM's MRS/MSR descriptions carry the system
# register as an IMMEDIATE — the 15-bit encoding, unexpanded — so there is
# no register operand on that side for any member of this family; the
# section header above says what a `phantom` verdict means here.
#
# REG_TLS is TPIDR_EL0, and `mrs xN, tpidr_el0` is how every thread-local
# access on AArch64 begins, so this is the member with real dynamic weight.
# REG_FLAGS is NZCV reached as a system register rather than as the
# condition flags; the tracer folds it onto the same id it uses for the
# flag writes of ordinary arithmetic, which is right — `msr nzcv, x0` does
# define what a later `b.eq` tests, and a consumer that kept the two apart
# would miss the edge.
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +flags
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +tls
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +flags
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +tls

# SME ZA tile and slice indexing, where the two decoders number the
# accumulator differently.  Same zero-weight SME space.  `zero {za}` names
# the WHOLE array rather than a tile, so it keys on the bare `+za`: there
# Capstone reports the array write and LLVM has no register operand at all,
# because it models the tile list as an immediate mask — the same shape as
# the system-register block above, in the SME register file.
aarch64 R-rd-phantom zero +sysfpen,za#
aarch64 R-wr-phantom zero +za
aarch64 R-wr-phantom zero +za#

# The general system-register space (REG_SYS): AArch64 MRS/MSR/MRRS/MSRR and
# the RISC-V Zicsr accesses.  The boundary reports the register the
# instruction moves; LLVM's description of these instructions has no
# register operand for it at all.
#
# THESE LINES DO NOT RECORD A COMPARISON THAT PASSED.  They record one that
# structurally cannot happen, and the distinction matters because the two
# look identical from inside an allowlist.  LLVM carries the CSR selector as
# the 12-bit immediate the encoding spells, so `sys` can never appear in
# LLVM's register sets for any Zicsr instruction, for any subtarget, for any
# normalisation -- `--hex=73700110` (`csrci sstatus, 2`) gives boundary
# RD{sys} WR{sys} against LLVM RD{-} WR{r0}, and there is nothing on the
# LLVM side to fold `sys` onto.  No entry below should be read as evidence
# that the tracer records the CSR dependency correctly; this gate has no
# opinion on that.
#
# The instrument that DOES have one is tools/implicit_audit.py, whose
# riscv64 expectations come from the Sail model rather than from a second
# disassembler and so do not need LLVM to name a register.  Twenty-two of
# its CSR rows assert the access at the REG_SYS / REG_FCSR / REG_VCTRL
# granularity the trace expresses, and they turn red if the boundary stops
# reporting it.  That is where a reviewer should look, not here.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrc +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrci +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrr +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrc +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrci +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrs +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrsi +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrw +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrwi +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrs +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrsi +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom rdcycle +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom rdinstret +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom rdtime +sys
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrc +sys
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrci +sys

# RISC-V `frm`: the dynamic rounding mode every FP operation encoded with
# rm = 111 reads.  Sail records it; LLVM models no `frm` use.

# Vector-register differences.  On AArch64 these are the SME multi-vector
# register LISTS, and the cause is OURS rather than Capstone's: `bfmls
# za.h[w8, 7, vgx4], { z0.h - z3.h }, { z4.h - z7.h }` has NINE operands --
# the ZA tile plus eight Z registers -- and Capstone reports all nine
# correctly (its own NUM_AARCH64_OPS is 16), but qemu_plugin_insn_info
# carries QEMU_PLUGIN_INSN_DETAIL_MAX_OPS == 8, so the ninth is truncated at
# the boundary and z7 never reaches the register sets.  Raising the cap
# widens every decode struct in the plugin by ~48 bytes per added slot, for
# a feature no guest the tracer runs can execute -- weight zero -- so the
# cap stands and the truncation is recorded, here and in
# docs/limitations.rst.  Check it with `cstool -d aarch64 1f10e5c1`: the
# operand count is 9, and z7 is the one that does not fit.
# On RISC-V they are the vector
# DESTINATION read: under tail-undisturbed and mask-undisturbed policy the
# elements `vd` does not write keep their old values, so `vd` really is a
# source; the Sail model records exactly this and LLVM does not.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vlm.v +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmadc.vi +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmadc.vv +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmadc.vx +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmand.mm +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmandn.mm +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmmv.m +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmnand.mm +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmnor.mm +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmnot.m +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmor.mm +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmorn.mm +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsbc.vv +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsbc.vx +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsbf.m +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmseq.vi +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmseq.vv +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmseq.vx +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsgt.vi +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsgt.vx +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsgtu.vi +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsgtu.vx +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsle.vi +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsle.vv +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsle.vx +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsleu.vi +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsleu.vv +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmsleu.vx +v#
riscv64 R-rd-phantom vmslt.vv +v#

# MIPS DSPControl: `bposge32` branches on the DSPControl `pos` field, and
# the boundary now reports the read.  LLVM models no DSPControl use.
mipsel R-rd-phantom bposge32 +dsp

# MIPS MSA control registers reached through CTCMSA, and the one place the
# two decoders genuinely disagree about this bank once the spelling is
# folded (see norm_mips): LLVM's MCInstrDesc carries the control register as
# a USE of the instruction, so it appears in LLVM's read set and never in
# its write set, while Capstone and the tracer both make it the DEF that a
# "copy TO control register" instruction plainly writes.  Both entries below
# are that single difference seen from its two sides.  Capstone is the
# correct one and the tracer follows it, so nothing here needs repairing.
#
# The sibling CFCMSA -- "copy FROM control register" -- has no such
# disagreement and no entry: it agreed the moment the tool could tell the
# two spellings apart.
mipsel R-rd-missing ctcmsa +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom ctcmsa +cop#

# =====================================================================
# 7. The x86 ModRM.rm space, reached once rm was swept as well as reg.
#
#    ModRM has two register fields and both of them carry opcode.  Sweeping
#    reg (block 6 above) left rm pinned -- to %rcx on the register tail, and
#    to whichever addressing form each memory tail happened to spell.  Three
#    things sat outside the sweep because of it: the other seven register
#    choices at mod = 11, the mod = 11 opcode-extension columns where rm IS
#    the instruction (`0f 01`, `0f ae`, `0f c7`, the x87 escapes), and the
#    base-register addressing forms at mod != 11.
#
#    That was not a hypothetical gap.  `callq *%rcx` was inside the swept
#    space and `callq *%rdx` was not, and it was the second one that exposed
#    a boundary defect deleting a real architectural read from every indirect
#    call -- a defect twelve entries in this file had grown justifications
#    around.  A pinned field is not a smaller sweep, it is an unmeasured one.
#
#    Sweeping rm multiplies the x86 population by eight, 245,760 -> 1,966,080
#    encodings, and moves the share of a traced x86 population lying inside
#    the swept space from 53.8% to 62.8% of distinct encodings and from 33.6%
#    to 58.8% by dynamic weight.  757 signatures follow.  Two of them are
#    real Capstone defects and are labelled as such -- (h) and (i) -- and one
#    more, (c3), is a decode-table gap that could reach a trace.  The rest is
#    the prefix-cutting artifact that already dominates x86 here, arriving in
#    bulk because the wider sweep puts more opcodes behind the same prefixes.
# =====================================================================

# (a) The subtarget gap.  Forty-six of these forty-eight are one byte string:
# a REX prefix followed by a SECOND REX prefix, `48 40 ...`, which sweeping
# rm reaches because 0x40 is an opcode value and the rm loop now supplies the
# eight tails behind it.  Hardware honours only the REX immediately preceding
# the opcode and ignores the earlier one, so `48 40 f8` is a three-byte CLC;
# Capstone decodes it that way and LLVM refuses the encoding outright.  Named
# exactly, as every gap signature must be.
#
# The two that are not double-REX are the 8087 and 287 legacy no-ops,
# `db e0` FENI and `db e4` FSETPM, which sit at rm = 0 and rm = 4 in an x87
# escape column whose rm = 1 member (`db e1` FDISI) was already reachable and
# already listed above.  Capstone keeps the historical mnemonics; LLVM models
# no instruction there.
x86_64 D-cs-only bnd callq
x86_64 D-cs-only bnd jge
x86_64 D-cs-only bnd jrcxz
x86_64 D-cs-only bnd lretl
x86_64 D-cs-only bnd lretq
x86_64 D-cs-only bnd retq
x86_64 D-cs-only callq
x86_64 D-cs-only clc
x86_64 D-cs-only cld
x86_64 D-cs-only cli
x86_64 D-cs-only cmc
x86_64 D-cs-only enter
x86_64 D-cs-only fcoml
x86_64 D-cs-only fcoms
x86_64 D-cs-only feni8087_nop
x86_64 D-cs-only ficoml
x86_64 D-cs-only ficoms
x86_64 D-cs-only fistl
x86_64 D-cs-only fists
x86_64 D-cs-only fsetpm
x86_64 D-cs-only fstl
x86_64 D-cs-only hlt
x86_64 D-cs-only inb
x86_64 D-cs-only inl
x86_64 D-cs-only int
x86_64 D-cs-only int3
x86_64 D-cs-only iretl

# (b) The two decoders cut a prefix-heavy byte string into different
# instructions.  This is the family that already dominates the x86 residual,
# and it is the overwhelming majority of what sweeping rm adds: 570 of the
# 757 new signatures.  Nothing new happens in it -- LLVM's X86 disassembler
# stops after consuming a prefix run and returns it as a standalone
# instruction (`llvm{es}`, `llvm{repne}`, `llvm{rex64}`), Capstone consumes
# the whole byte string -- but the wider sweep puts far more opcodes behind
# those prefixes.  Once the two sides are describing different instructions
# every downstream class follows: the size disagrees, the memory operand
# disagrees, and the register sets are two different instructions' register
# sets.  A signature here is a length disagreement wearing a metadata
# disagreement's clothes, and reading it as a dependency defect would be a
# mistake.

# (c) LLVM decodes and Capstone does not.  Three separate reasons, kept
# apart because only one of them says anything about the tracer.
#
# (c1) The mirror image of (b): the byte string is a prefix run that LLVM is
# willing to hand back as an instruction and Capstone rejects rather than
# return a partial decode.  Same artifact, other side.
x86_64 D-llvm-only addr32
x86_64 D-llvm-only cs
x86_64 D-llvm-only data16
x86_64 D-llvm-only ds
x86_64 D-llvm-only es
x86_64 D-llvm-only fs
x86_64 D-llvm-only gs
x86_64 D-llvm-only rex64
x86_64 D-llvm-only ss

# (c2) Prefix TOLERANCE, not a missing encoding.  Capstone decodes each of
# these correctly in its canonical form -- `f30f1efa` is endbr64, `f30f1efb`
# is endbr32, `f30f01ea` is saveprevssp, `f30f01e8` is setssbsy -- and
# refuses the same instruction when a redundant `66` is prepended, which is
# the shape the `{0x66,0xf3}` prefix pair in the sweep produces.  The
# canonical encodings are the ones a trace contains, and they are decoded, so
# nothing here reaches the tracer.
x86_64 D-llvm-only endbr32
x86_64 D-llvm-only endbr64
x86_64 D-llvm-only saveprevssp
x86_64 D-llvm-only setssbsy

# (c3) Encodings Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7's hand-maintained x86 tables do not
# carry at all.  Verified by decoding each in its canonical, unprefixed form:
# `--hex=0f01e8` (SERIALIZE), `--hex=0f01c6` (WRMSRNS), `--hex=0f01fd`
# (RDPRU), `--hex=0f01fe` (INVLPGB), `--hex=0f01ff` (TLBSYNC), `--hex=0f01c7`
# (PBNDKB) and the F3/F2-prefixed SEV-SNP, TDX and user-interrupt members all
# return ok=0 from the boundary while LLVM 18.1.3 names them.  Two more are
# encoding-space rather than table scope: `26d5112233`, where `d5` is the APX
# REX2 prefix, and `620a8d000000`, an EVEX form reaching the extended
# register file -- LLVM models both, Capstone does not.
#
# This is the one part of the gap that could reach a trace: an unprefixed
# SERIALIZE or WRMSRNS is a real instruction a real binary may contain, and
# the boundary would hand the tracer nothing for it (the plugin logs it to
# the unknown-mnemonic sidecar, so it is a known unknown rather than a silent
# corruption).  Recorded here rather than filed upstream by maintainer
# decision; when a Capstone bump adds these tables the lines go dead and the
# gate turns red, which is the tripwire.
x86_64 D-llvm-only andb
x86_64 D-llvm-only hreset
x86_64 D-llvm-only invlpgb
x86_64 D-llvm-only pbndkb
x86_64 D-llvm-only psmash
x86_64 D-llvm-only pvalidate
x86_64 D-llvm-only rdmsrlist
x86_64 D-llvm-only rdpru
x86_64 D-llvm-only rmpadjust
x86_64 D-llvm-only rmpquery
x86_64 D-llvm-only rmpupdate
x86_64 D-llvm-only seamops
x86_64 D-llvm-only seamret
x86_64 D-llvm-only serialize
x86_64 D-llvm-only tdcall
x86_64 D-llvm-only testui
x86_64 D-llvm-only tlbsync
x86_64 D-llvm-only uiret
x86_64 D-llvm-only vpshufb
x86_64 D-llvm-only wrmsrlist
x86_64 D-llvm-only wrmsrns
x86_64 D-llvm-only xsusldtrk

# (d) LLVM names a segment register as a read whenever a segment-override
# prefix is present and Capstone does not.  In 64-bit mode ES, CS, SS and DS
# have no base and the override is architecturally inert, which is why
# Capstone reports no operand for it; the tracer's dependency model has
# nothing to record either.  The pre-existing `+seg` entries above are the
# same difference seen from the phantom side, on the RIP-relative tail that

# (e) x87 control and status words, and the implicit stack top.  LLVM
# attaches FPCW as a use and FPSW as a def to essentially the whole x87
# space, because rounding and precision control are inputs to every
# arithmetic result and the status word carries the condition codes;
# Capstone reports the stack registers the instruction names and neither
# control word.  The same difference is already listed for the x87 members
# the ModRM.reg sweep reached (`faddl +fpcw`, `fnstsw +fpsw`); sweeping rm
# reaches the mod = 11 column of the escape opcodes, which is the register-
# to-register half of the same space.  FTST and FXAM are the two where the
# arrow points the other way -- Capstone names the ST(0) read those two
# genuinely perform and LLVM does not.
#
# THE mod = 11 REGISTER FORMS ARRIVED LATE, and not because anything moved:
# until the fields-layer work of #169 the tool spelled Capstone's `st(1)`
# and LLVM's `st1` as different tokens, so every x87 register operand was
# an unmapped name and the register sets were never compared at all.  One
# spelling (norm_x86) made the comparison happen, and this is what it
# found -- which is the whole argument for folding a spelling rather than
# allowlisting it: the rows were always true, they were just unreadable.
#
# The FCMOVcc rows read against a CORRECTED boundary.  Capstone reports
# that family with its two operand roles exchanged -- ST(0) read, ST(i)
# written, for an instruction that moves ST(i) into ST(0) -- and
# cap_x86_is_cmov in disas/capstone.c now forces the destination
# read-write and the source read.  What is left is LLVM's side: its
# description names neither the implicit ST(0) read nor the ST(0) write,
# so the tracer is the one that is right in both directions.
#
# ONE ROW HERE IS A DEFECT, NOT A DIFFERENCE, and is recorded as one:
# `R-wr-phantom fxam +st#`.  Capstone's implicit regs_write for FXAM
# (`--hex=d9e5`) contains ST(0), and the SDM is explicit that FXAM only
# classifies the value in ST(0) and sets C0/C2/C3 -- it does not modify
# it.  The trace therefore carries a definition of ST(0) that never
# happened, a WAW edge against whatever wrote it last.  Left uncorrected
# deliberately: it is one instruction, essentially absent from compiled
# code (x87 classify), and the boundary already carries as many
# hand-written corrections as it should before the execution-derived
# capture replaces the source of all of them.  Named so it cannot be read
# as vouched for; owed upstream with the CMOV/FCMOV report.
x86_64 R-rd-missing fistl +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing fistl +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing fistl +es
x86_64 R-rd-missing fistl +ss
x86_64 R-rd-missing fists +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing fists +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing fists +es
x86_64 R-rd-missing fists +ss
x86_64 R-rd-missing fld +fpcw
x86_64 R-rd-missing fld1 +fpcw
x86_64 R-rd-missing fldz +fpcw
x86_64 R-rd-missing fst +fpcw
x86_64 R-rd-missing fstl +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing fstl +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing fstl +es
x86_64 R-rd-missing fstl +ss
x86_64 R-rd-missing fxch +fpcw
x86_64 R-wr-missing fnop +fpsw
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fcmovb +st#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fcmovbe +st#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fcmove +st#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fcmovnb +st#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fcmovnbe +st#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fcmovne +st#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fcmovnu +st#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fcmovu +st#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fstp +st#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fxam +st#

# (f) Implicit general-purpose operands on the `0f 01` mod = 11 column.
# CLZERO reads RAX, MONITORX reads RAX/RCX/RDX, MWAITX reads RAX/RCX/RBX,
# PCONFIG reads and writes RAX/RBX/RCX/RDX and writes the flags, RDPKRU
# writes EAX/EDX and reads ECX, WRPKRU reads all three.  LLVM's
# MCInstrDesc names them; Capstone returns no operands for any of these
# instructions.  The already-listed `umonitor +r#` and `umwait +r#` entries
# are the same hole one opcode over.  None of these instructions is emitted
# by a compiler for the subset the tracer targets, so the missing edges are
# unreachable in practice; the entry records the hole rather than pretending
# the two decoders agree.
x86_64 R-rd-missing pconfig +r#
x86_64 R-rd-missing rdpkru +r#
x86_64 R-rd-missing wrpkru +r#
x86_64 R-wr-missing pconfig +flags,r#
x86_64 R-wr-missing rdpkru +r#

# (g) mayLoad / mayStore on fences and transactional-memory ops.  These are
# LLVM SCHEDULING properties, not semantics -- "do not move memory across
# me", which is exactly what a fence is for -- and LLVM sets them on the
# whole barrier space.  None of these instructions has a memory operand and
# Capstone reports none.  Identical to the family already listed for `emms`,
# `femms` and `incsspd`, and to the aarch64 barrier block earlier in this
# file.  LFENCE, MFENCE and SFENCE join it here because they live at
# `0f ae /5 /6 /7` with mod = 11 and rm = 0, which no pinned rm could reach.
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none lfence load store
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none mfence load store
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none saveprevssp load store
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none setssbsy load store
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none sfence load store
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none xabort load store
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none xend load store

# (h) A REAL CAPSTONE LENGTH DEFECT, recorded as one rather than as a
# modelling difference.  `66 48 c2 11 22` is `data16 rex.W ret $0x2211`, five
# bytes.  LLVM 18.1.3 says five and GNU binutils 2.42 says five; Capstone
# 6.0.0-Alpha7 says SEVEN while printing the correct 16-bit immediate, so its
# length accounting and its printer disagree with each other.  `RET imm16`
# takes a fixed 16-bit immediate whatever the operand size, so there is no
# reading of the encoding under which seven is right.  The trigger is
# specifically `66` followed by REX.W: `--hex=66c2112233` is 4 (correct),
# `--hex=663ec2112233` is 5 (correct), `--hex=48c2112233` is 4 (correct),
# `--hex=6648c211223344` is 7 (wrong).  It is the same defect already
# recorded above for `66`-prefixed JMP rel, where Capstone consumes a rel32
# where the operand-size prefix selects rel16 (`--hex=663ee911223344`).
# The tracer does not take instruction boundaries from Capstone -- QEMU's
# translator owns those -- so this corrupts disassembly text, not the trace.
# Not filed upstream, by maintainer decision; the line goes dead on a
# Capstone bump and the gate turns red.
x86_64 D-size-mismatch retw

# (i) A REAL CAPSTONE MISIDENTIFICATION, and the reason this signature is
# NOT filed under (g) even though its class looks identical.  `f3 0f 01 ee`
# is CLUI and `f3 0f 01 ef` is STUI -- LLVM 18.1.3 and GNU binutils 2.42 both
# say so -- but Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7 names them `rdpkru` and `wrpkru`, the
# unprefixed forms at the same ModRM byte.  It consumes the F3 (it reports
# size 4) and then does not dispatch on it, while handling F3 correctly two
# columns over at `f3 0f 01 ea` SAVEPREVSSP and `f3 0f 01 e8` SETSSBSY.  The
# consequence for a trace is not cosmetic: RDPKRU writes EAX and EDX and
# reads ECX, CLUI touches no general-purpose register at all, so a CLUI would
# be recorded with three register edges it does not have.  The signature
# lands in the memory class only because LLVM's CLUI carries the barrier
# mayLoad/mayStore of (g) and Capstone's RDPKRU does not -- the class is an
# accident of which instruction each side thinks it is looking at.
# Reproduce with `--hex=f30f01ee` and `--hex=f30f01ef`.  Not filed upstream,
# by maintainer decision.
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none rdpkru load store
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none wrpkru load store

# (j) Capstone reports the stack-pointer write on the near call and return
# forms and LLVM's MCInstrDesc records no implicit def for them.  Capstone is
# the correct one and the tracer follows Capstone, so this is LLVM being less
# precise, not a hole.  Already listed for `callq`, `retq`, `bnd callq` and
# `bnd retq`; these are the `notrack` and 16-bit spellings the rm sweep adds.
x86_64 R-wr-phantom bnd notrack callq +r#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom bnd retw +r#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom notrack callq +r#

# --- x86_64: the residual the WIDENED sweep reaches.
#
# The sweep gained three dimensions at once -- REX as a full 16-value
# field instead of the single `48` spelling, ModRM.mod = 01 / 10, and the
# whole VEX space, which had never been emitted at all.  Encodings tried
# went 1,966,080 -> 154,337,280 and the share of a traced x86 population
# lying inside the swept space went 62.76% -> 98.07% of distinct
# encodings, 58.82% -> 99.66% by dynamic weight.  What follows is what
# that newly-reached space disagrees about, split by CAUSE rather than
# recorded as one undifferentiated block -- three of the four causes
# describe no defect and the fourth is a real one, and collapsing them
# would bury it.
#
# The x86 policy stated further up still holds: PIN remains the dynamic
# authority for x86 and this block exists so that a Capstone or LLVM bump
# shows up as a gate failure rather than as silent trace corruption.
#
# CAUSE 1 (748 entries) -- REX IS NOT THE LAST PREFIX.  Every sample here
# carries a REX byte followed by another prefix, or a second REX.  Intel
# SDM Vol. 2A 2.2.1: "Only one REX prefix is allowed per instruction" and
# it "must immediately precede the opcode byte or the escape opcode
# byte"; a REX anywhere else is simply not a REX and the architecture
# gives the byte no meaning there.  Capstone tolerates the sequence and
# folds it, LLVM either rejects it (`D-cs-only`) or stops at the stray
# prefix and returns it as a one- or two-byte instruction of its own
# (`D-size-mismatch`, and the register/flow families downstream of that
# length disagreement).  No assembler emits this and it is not a decode
# defect on either side -- it is two decoders resolving an encoding the
# architecture does not define.  VERIFIED against the reference
# population: 0 of 47,512 distinct encodings and 0 of 1.64 G dynamic
# instructions carry a REX that is not the last prefix.
x86_64 D-cs-only adcw
x86_64 D-cs-only addw
x86_64 D-cs-only andw
x86_64 D-cs-only bnd ja
x86_64 D-cs-only bnd jae
