# CAUSE 2 (73 entries) -- LLVM RETURNS A PREFIX AS ITS OWN INSTRUCTION.
# Almost all of these are `lock` with REX.W: LLVM's MC disassembler
# decodes `f0` as the one-byte LOCK pseudo-instruction and stops, where
# the boundary decodes the whole fused RMW.  Every register, memory and
# length finding on these encodings is downstream of that single
# convention difference.  The identical family for the non-REX.W
# spellings (`lock cmpxchgl`, `lock xaddl`, ...) is already recorded
# above; only the mnemonic changed, because REX.W renames the
# instruction and the signature key carries the mnemonic.  The remainder
# are `66`+`f3`+REX.W sequences carrying two mandatory prefixes at once,
# where the two decoders pick different members of the mandatory-prefix
# column (`lfence` vs `incsspq`, `xsave` vs `ptwriteq`, `clwb` vs
# `xsaveopt64`).  Also 0 occurrences in the reference population.
x86_64 D-llvm-only aandq
x86_64 D-llvm-only incsspq
x86_64 D-llvm-only ptwriteq
x86_64 D-llvm-only rdfsbaseq
x86_64 D-llvm-only rdgsbaseq
x86_64 D-llvm-only rdsspq
x86_64 D-llvm-only wrfsbaseq
x86_64 D-llvm-only wrgsbaseq
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock btcq
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock btrq
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock btsq
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock cmpxchg16b
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock cmpxchgq
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock decq
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock incq
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock negq
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock xaddq
x86_64 D-size-mismatch lock xchgq
x86_64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none tpause load store
x86_64 M-miss-load wrussq
x86_64 R-rd-missing clwb +r#
x86_64 R-rd-missing lfence +r#

# CAUSE 3 (136 entries) -- SEGMENT AND DIRECTION-FLAG MODELLING.  Reached
# because mod = 10 puts a string-op opcode where the ModRM byte goes, so
# a prefix byte in the opcode slot now produces real segment-overridden
# string forms.  Two sub-shapes, both benign, both checked:
#
#   `+seg`: LLVM's X86 memory operand always carries a segment-register
#   slot, so an explicit override makes LLVM report a segment read.  For
#   the overrides here -- ES (`26`), CS, SS and DS (`3e`) -- 64-bit mode
#   forces the segment base to zero and the override contributes nothing
#   to the effective address, so the boundary is right not to record a
#   dependency.  THIS IS NOT THE SAME AS DROPPING FS/GS: those two do
#   have a live base, and the tracer records them.  Verified directly --
#   `--hex=64488b042500000000` (`movq %fs:0, %rax`) gives boundary
#   RD{seg}, LLVM RD{seg} and fields SRC{REG_SEG3}, and the `%gs:` form
#   gives REG_SEG4.  The benign family and the family that would have
#   been a real defect are distinguishable, and only the benign one is
#   here.
#
#   `+df` USED TO BE THE SECOND SUB-SHAPE, and it was a gate blind spot
#   rather than a difference.  LLVM reports the direction flag as a read
#   on the string ops, the boundary reports the whole flags word, and
#   `norm_x86()` folded neither onto the other -- so the two tokens stood
#   opposite each other permanently, could not cancel, and could not
#   fail.  The normaliser now folds `df` onto `flags`, which is what the
#   tracer itself does (both land in the one REG_FLAGS slot), so every
#   `+df` qualifier in this block is gone: what is left is either the
#   residual that was standing behind it, respelled in place, or nothing
#   at all.  The DIRECTION FLAG section at the end of this file carries
#   the measurement.
x86_64 D-llvm-only cmovel
x86_64 D-llvm-only cmoveq
x86_64 D-llvm-only cmovew
x86_64 D-size-mismatch xacquire lock xchgb
x86_64 D-size-mismatch xacquire lock xchgl
x86_64 D-size-mismatch xrelease lock xchgb
x86_64 D-size-mismatch xrelease lock xchgl

# CAUSE 4 -- CAPSTONE'S VEX OPERAND DETAIL IS WRONG.  These ARE defects,
# and they were invisible for as long as the sweep emitted no VEX byte.
# They are the same bug class already worked around at the boundary for
# the SSE spellings (`cap_x86_is_extract_store`, `cap_x86_is_move_family`
# in disas/capstone.c).  Observed with Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7 and LLVM 18.
#
# THE MEMORY-DIRECTION HALF IS NOW FIXED, and the proof is that its
# signatures are gone from this file rather than described in it.  The
# boundary was extended to cover the VEX spellings the `v`-strip did not
# reach -- `cap_x86_is_extract_store` now takes the `extract` stem
# (VEXTRACTF128/I128 and the EVEX lane extracts) and VCVTPS2PH, and
# `cap_x86_is_move_family` now takes the masked moves VMASKMOV{PS,PD} /
# VPMASKMOV{D,Q} and the AVX-512 mask moves KMOV{B,W,D,Q}, whose store
# forms are separated from their identically-spelled load forms by the
# access pattern rather than by name.  `cap_x86_is_erased_mem_load`
# restores both halves of VBROADCASTI128's erased operand pair.  Twenty-
# two entries (M-extra-load / M-miss-store on the extract, convert, mask
# and masked-move families; M-miss-load, R-wr-missing and both Z-access0
# rows on vbroadcasti128) went DEAD in the same change and were removed;
# `x86_64 W-wr-added vbroadcasti128 +REG_VEC#` went dead in
# isaxcheck_fixups.txt for the same reason and went with them.  Direction
# was taken from QEMU's own translator (target/i386/tcg/decode-new.c.inc
# operand shapes and emit.c.inc helpers), not from LLVM agreement -- see
# the per-family comments in disas/capstone.c.
#
# WHAT REMAINS HERE, and why each is not the same thing:
#
#   `M-miss-load vmaskmov{ps,pd}` / `vpmaskmov{d,q}` are the four entries
#   the fix ADDED, and they describe no defect.  LLVM marks the masked
#   STORE forms mayLoad=1 as well as mayStore=1; mayLoad is a SCHEDULING
#   property, not semantics, and this is the same artifact already
#   documented for the AArch64 barrier/hint family further up.  QEMU's
#   helper settles it: `helper_vpmaskmovd_st` in target/i386/ops_sse.h
#   contains cpu_stl_data_ra() and nothing else -- the instruction only
#   ever writes.  The boundary is right and LLVM is conservative.
#
#   THE STMXCSR AND SETcc MEMORY DIRECTIONS ARE NOW FIXED TOO, in
#   `cap_x86_is_lost_mem_store`, and so is VCVTPD2PSX's erasure (folded
#   into `cap_x86_is_erased_mem_load` beside VBROADCASTI128, whose shape
#   it shares exactly) and the gather family's lost mask write (in
#   `cap_x86_is_gather`).  Forty-four entries went dead in that change and
#   were removed -- 28 SETcc rows (M-extra-load and M-miss-store on the
#   fourteen condition codes Capstone got wrong), 4 on stmxcsr/vstmxcsr,
#   4 on vcvtpd2psx and 8 R-wr-missing rows across the gathers -- and
#   `x86_64 W-wr-added vcvtpd2psx +REG_VEC#` went dead in
#   isaxcheck_fixups.txt with them.  Direction came from QEMU's translator
#   in every case; see the per-family comments in disas/capstone.c.
#
#   THE WHOLE x87 ESCAPE SPACE IS NOW FIXED, in `cap_x86_x87_mem_access`,
#   and it is the case that shows how far a justification will travel on
#   its own.  Twelve of the eighteen x87 memory-DESTINATION forms were
#   reported READ -- fnstcw, fsts, fstps, fstl, fstpl, fists, fistl,
#   fistps, fistpl, fisttps, fisttpl, fisttpll -- and FRSTOR, whose
#   108-byte memory operand it only ever reads, was reported WRITE.
#   Twenty-six entries went dead and were removed.  They had been sitting
#   in THREE different places under THREE justifications, none of which
#   described them: five pairs under "the two decoders cut a prefix-heavy
#   byte string into different instructions" (section 6(b)) where both
#   decoders in fact printed the same instruction; four pairs plus the two
#   frstor rows under "the x87 integer load/store forms where the two
#   disagree about direction" (section 6(d)), which names the bucket and
#   asserts nothing; and three pairs inside the x86_64 baseline block,
#   which triages nothing by construction.  No x87 memory operand is
#   read-modify-write, so all eighteen store spellings and the FPU-
#   environment loads are named at the boundary and the repair is a no-op
#   on the six Capstone already gets right.  Ground truth is gen_x87() in
#   target/i386/tcg/translate.c, keyed by the same
#   `op = ((b & 7) << 3) | ModRM.reg` the boundary comment tabulates.
#
#   `M-miss-store ldmxcsr` / `vldmxcsr` SURVIVED that change and are NOT
#   the stmxcsr defect wearing a different name.  LDMXCSR is a LOAD --
#   `X86_OP_ENTRYr(LDMXCSR, E,d)`, `r` for source -- and Capstone reports
#   its MEM operand READ correctly.  What LLVM asserts is mayStore=1,
#   because the instruction writes MXCSR, which is processor state and not
#   memory; that is the same scheduling-property artifact as the masked
#   moves above, not a direction disagreement.  The distinction is
#   load-bearing: ldmxcsr and stmxcsr differ only in ModRM.reg (0F AE /2
#   versus /3) and share the `mxcsr` stem, so a stem-matched repair would
#   have inverted the healthy one, which is why the boundary matches both
#   spellings exactly.
#
#   access == 0 erasures that are STILL not repaired: the vpermil2pd/ps
#   VEX.L=1 forms.  The earlier text here said the "five-operand forms
#   lose their MEM operand"; that was measured and is wrong on both
#   counts.  Operand count is not the discriminator -- VEX.L is.  Every
#   VEX.L=0 form is reported CORRECTLY (MEM READ, destination register
#   WRITE) at both VEX.W values and with either a memory or a register
#   source; every VEX.L=1 form comes back with access == 0 on all five
#   operands, memory and registers alike.  The one exception is uglier
#   than a plain erasure: the VEX.W=1, VEX.L=1 REGISTER form
#   (`--hex=c4e3f549c81a`) gets a three-operand access pattern laid over
#   its five-operand array, so the immediate is marked READ and a SOURCE
#   register is marked WRITE -- that is what the `R-wr-phantom vpermil2*`
#   rows record, and an `access == 0` gate cannot reach it because those
#   operands' access is not zero.
#
#   Left unrepaired deliberately, and the residual is bounded: the
#   plugin's own operand walker already recovers the whole register set
#   (the `W-rd-added` / `W-wr-added vpermil2*` rows in
#   isaxcheck_fixups.txt assert exactly that), so the only state actually
#   dropped is the load lane and the address dependency on the L=1 memory
#   forms.  Against that, a repair here would have to be positional --
#   "the last operand is the destination, the rest are sources" -- rather
#   than the access-flag rule every other repair in disas/capstone.c uses,
#   because three of the five zero-access registers are sources and
#   marking them all WRITE the way `cap_x86_is_erased_mem_load` does would
#   fabricate three phantom writes; and it would still leave the
#   misaligned form's phantom write standing, so it could not be a
#   complete fix by construction.  QEMU implements no VPERMIL2 at all
#   (there is no entry for VEX 0F3A 48/49 anywhere in decode-new.c.inc),
#   which means there is no translator ground truth to take the direction
#   from and, equally, that the instruction can never be translated and
#   can never reach a trace -- the same standing as kmov*.  It is x86
#   work with a written shape, not a gap nobody has looked at.
#
# MEASURED EXPOSURE of the families fixed above, against the four x86
# reference traces (1.64 G dynamic correct-path instructions, 47,512
# distinct encodings; extraction method under cst_runs/tracesweep):
# ZERO correct-path executions for every one of them, and zero wrong-path
# too.  vextracti128 is the only one that appears at all -- 8 distinct
# encodings filling 28 template slots in the two perlbench traces, every
# one of them the REGISTER-destination form that this fix does not touch,
# and none of them ever retiring.  vextractf128, vcvtps2ph, vmaskmov*,
# vpmaskmov*, kmov* and vbroadcasti128 do not appear at all.
#
# THE SAME MEASUREMENT for the four families fixed after them, taken the
# same way and re-derived rather than inherited.  Every one is ZERO on the
# correct path, so none of them moves a byte of any current reference
# trace either; what they carry is wrong-path weight and correctness
# banked for workloads not yet run.
#
#   stmxcsr / vstmxcsr   2 distinct encodings, both vstmxcsr (the
#                        non-VEX spelling never appears), cp=0,
#                        wp=2,826 -- all of it on `c5f8ae5dd8`.
#   SETcc to memory      2 distinct encodings, both `setb` (`0f9245af`
#                        and `0f9245b3`), cp=0, wp=427.  These are the
#                        only memory-form SETcc in the population that
#                        used one of the fourteen BROKEN condition
#                        codes; the fourteen encodings that used sete
#                        or setne (cp=120, wp=11,554) were already
#                        correct and the fix leaves them bit-identical.
#   vcvtpd2psx           absent -- 0 distinct encodings.
#   gather family        absent -- 0 distinct encodings, all eight
#                        mnemonics.
#
# vpermil2pd/ps, which is NOT fixed, is likewise absent.
#
# For scale, VEX as a whole is 2.59% of dynamic weight there and
# is dominated by vzeroupper (20,661,182 executions, 1.26%) and the
# vmovdq* moves, all of which were already correct.  The fix therefore
# moves no byte of any current reference trace; its exposure is an
# AVX2-heavy workload, and for the kmov* half specifically there is no
# exposure at all under this QEMU -- the i386 TCG front end implements
# no EVEX, so a KMOV cannot be translated and cannot reach a trace.
x86_64 D-llvm-only ldtilecfg
x86_64 D-llvm-only sttilecfg
x86_64 D-llvm-only tcmmimfp16ps
x86_64 D-llvm-only tcmmrlfp16ps
x86_64 D-llvm-only tdpbf16ps

# --- mipsel: the residual the `sa`-swept sweep reaches.
#
# `sa` stopped being a three-value fill and became an exhaustively swept
# opcode field (see sweep_mips), which opened the SPECIAL3 selector
# columns -- BSHFL and the whole DSP ASE -- for the first time.  Three
# causes, and they are NOT the same thing even though two of them share
# a signature class.
#
# CAUSE 1 -- LLVM MARKS THE DSP ARITHMETIC OPS mayLoad AND mayStore.
# These are register-to-register operations on packed data; they touch no
# memory.  LLVM's MIPS tablegen gives the DSP ASE entries conservative
# memory flags because they read and write the DSPControl register as
# implicit state, and mayLoad/mayStore is how that side effect is spelled.
# Capstone models no memory operand, which is right.
#
# THIS CLASS IS A KNOWN COLLISION HAZARD and was checked rather than
# assumed: `M-llvm-mem-cs-none` is also what a MISIDENTIFIED instruction
# produces (the two decoders naming different instructions and therefore
# disagreeing about memory).  Verified for all 38 members below -- the
# mnemonic Capstone prints and the mnemonic LLVM prints are byte-identical
# in every one, so no member of this block is a misidentification wearing
# the benign family's signature.
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none absq_s.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none absq_s.w load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none addq_s.w load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none addsc load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none addu.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none addu_s.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none addwc load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgdu.eq.qb load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgdu.le.qb load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgdu.lt.qb load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgu.le.qb load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgu.lt.qb load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none mul.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none mul_s.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none muleq_s.w.phl load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none muleq_s.w.phr load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none muleu_s.ph.qbl load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none muleu_s.ph.qbr load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none mulq_rs.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none mulq_rs.w load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none mulq_s.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none mulq_s.w load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none pick.ph load
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none pick.qb load
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none precr.qb.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none precrq_rs.ph.w load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none precrqu_s.qb.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none rddsp load
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none shll_s.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none shll_s.w load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none shllv.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none shllv.qb load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none shllv_s.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none shllv_s.w load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none subq_s.w load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none subu.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none subu_s.ph load store
mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none wrdsp load store

# CAUSE 2 -- CAPSTONE MODELS DSPControl AND LLVM DOES NOT.  Here Capstone
# is the correct one and the tracer follows it.  RDDSP copies selected
# DSPControl fields into a GPR, WRDSP writes them, and MTHLIP reads the
# DSPControl `pos` field it then decrements; QEMU's own translator agrees
# (OPC_RDDSP / OPC_WRDSP / OPC_MTHLIP in target/mips/tcg/translate.c).
# LLVM keeps DSPControl out of its register sets entirely, so it is LLVM
# that is under-reporting.  Recorded so that if the tracer ever LOSES the
# DSPControl dependency these lines go dead and say so.
#
# RDDSP and WRDSP are also the two encodings the trace-driven cross-check
# flagged that this sweep could not previously build at all -- they live at
# sa = 18 and sa = 19.
mipsel R-rd-phantom mthlip +dsp
mipsel R-rd-phantom rddsp +dsp
mipsel R-wr-phantom wrdsp +dsp

# CAUSE 3 -- CLOSED.  DELIBERATELY LEFT AS A HEADING WITH NO ENTRIES.
#
# This block held three lines -- `R-rd-missing balign`, `precr_sra.ph.w`
# and `precr_sra_r.ph.w` -- recording a REAL DEFECT and not a convention
# difference: all three are read-modify-write on `rt`, Capstone reported the
# destination WRITE-only, and the architectural read was deleted all the way
# through to the recorded InsnFields.  It is fixed at the boundary, in
# `cap_mips_is_tied_dst()` (disas/capstone.c), and the entries are gone
# because the read now appears on both sides: `--hex=311c017c`
# (`balign $at, $zero, 3`) gives boundary RD{r0,r1} against LLVM RD{r0,r1}
# and fields SRC{REG_GPR1,REG_ZERO}, where it used to give boundary RD{r0}
# and fields SRC{REG_ZERO} with no read of $at at all.
#
# The heading stays because of the exposure measurement it carried, which
# the gate cannot re-derive: these three retire ZERO dynamic instructions
# across the reference mipsel population -- 1.36 G correct-path and 20.4 G
# wrong-path instructions over 58,526 distinct encodings, with no `balign`
# or `precr_sra*` encoding among them -- so the defect cost nothing
# measurable and the workload that would have paid for it is a DSP-using
# one.  A defect whose weight today is zero is still a defect, and that
# distinction is what this heading is for.

# --- x86_64: the residual the SEGMENT-OVERRIDE bases reach.
#
# CS (`2e`), DS (`3e`) and GS (`65`) joined FS (`64`) in the legacy base
# table.  The point of adding them is that the four are not one case:
# FS and GS carry a live segment base and the tracer records it, CS and DS
# have a base forced to zero in 64-bit mode and it records nothing.  With
# only FS swept, the two behaved identically inside the gate and the whole
# zero-base family was reachable only by the trace-driven cross-check --
# which is where it was in fact found, as 0.00307% of the dynamic weight of
# the reference traces, all of it kernel code in perlbench_system plus the
# `66 2e` multi-byte NOP padding.  Encodings 154,337,280 -> 171,048,960,
# 10.5 s -> 11.9 s at --jobs=16.
#
# CAUSE 3 (continued) -- ZERO-BASE SEGMENT OVERRIDE.  LLVM's X86 memory
# operand always carries a segment-register slot, so an explicit override
# makes LLVM report a read; for CS and DS in 64-bit mode the base is zero
# and contributes nothing to the effective address, so the boundary is
# right not to record a dependency.  The discriminator is checked and
# holds: of the 428 register findings this block adds, every one leads with
# `3e` or `2e` and NOT ONE leads with `64` or `65` -- the live-base
# overrides agree on both sides, so the benign family and the family that
# would have been a real defect stay distinguishable.
#
# GRANULARITY.  These rows name the SEGMENT REGISTER, not the bank.  They
# used to read `+seg`, because the signature key elided every bank
# index; the six segment registers are architecturally distinct registers, so that
# elision handed back exactly the conflation the split IDs exist to
# prevent -- one row justified for an inert override licensed all six, and
# a defect that swapped one segment for another keyed identically to the
# correct mapping.  The index is now elided only for an INDEXED FILE
# (generic_reg_is_indexed_file(), champsim_tracer_generic_ids.h), where the
# members are interchangeable and one defect would otherwise mint 32 rows.
#
# What the finer key states, and the sweep now enforces line by line: every
# `R-rd-missing` row below is `+cs`, `+ds`, `+es` or `+ss` -- the four whose base is zero in 64-bit mode -- and NOT
# ONE is `+fs` or `+gs`.  The live-base overrides agree on
# both sides.  Under `+seg` that discriminator could only be checked
# by hand, off to the side of the gate; it is now the allowlist itself.
x86_64 R-rd-missing adcq +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing adcq +ds

# CAUSE 1/2 (continued) -- the NOTRACK and BND spellings of the same
# prefix-ordering and prefix-split families already recorded above.  Their
# samples are `3e 40 f2` / `3e 40 f3` / `3e 40 40`: a REX byte that is not
# the last prefix, which the architecture does not define, so LLVM stops at
# the stray prefix (`rep` / `repne`) or rejects outright while Capstone
# folds.  `+df` appears here for the same reason it appears above -- the
# tracer's x86 normaliser has no direction-flag token, so LLVM's `df` read
# is dropped before the comparison rather than compared.
x86_64 D-cs-only notrack callq
x86_64 D-cs-only notrack jmp
# --- x86_64: the residual left once the DIRECTION FLAG became comparable.
#
# LLVM models DF as a register in its own right; Capstone models it as a bit
# of EFLAGS and names the whole word.  `norm_x86()` used to leave the two
# apart, and a token only one side can ever produce is not a comparison.
# Every string instruction carried a permanent `R-rd-missing <mnem> +df`
# beside a permanent `R-rd-phantom <mnem> +flags`, the pair could not cancel,
# and -- the part that made it a blind spot rather than noise -- the boundary
# could have LOST its flags read outright without moving either signature,
# because `df` would still have been the LLVM-only token and `flags`
# vanishing from the Capstone side is not what the phantom bucket is keyed
# on.  A defect in the one register every string instruction depends on was
# structurally invisible.
#
# `df` is now folded onto `flags`, which is what the tracer itself does (both
# land in the single REG_FLAGS slot), so the two sides meet.  Verify with
# `--hex=f3aa` (`rep stosb`): boundary RD{flags,r0,r1,r7} against LLVM
# RD{flags,r0,r7} -- the flags read cancels and only the REP counter is left
# over.  A lost flags read on a string instruction now surfaces as
# `R-rd-missing <mnem> +flags`, which nothing here allows.
#
# The fold retired 328 entries.  205 came back with the same class and
# mnemonic and `df` gone from the qualifier; those were edited in place, in
# the blocks that already justified them, so this section does not hold
# them.  123 were removed, and 80 of those are class/mnemonic pairs with no
# surviving signature at all -- the disagreement they recorded has ceased to
# exist, which is the measurement that says the fold closed something rather
# than renaming it.
#
# What follows is the residual those entries were standing in front of: two
# families already documented above, now visible without `df` in the way.
#
# (a) THE REP COUNTER.  Capstone reports %rCX as read and written by every
# rep-prefixed string instruction and LLVM's instruction description does not
# name it.  Capstone is the correct side -- REP counts down in rCX and the
# architecture makes that a real dependency -- so this is LLVM
# under-modelling, the tracer follows Capstone, and there is nothing on the
# tracer's side to repair.
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep insb +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep insl +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep insw +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep lodsb +r#

x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep lodsw +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep movsb +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep movsl +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep movsq +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep movsw +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep outsb +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep outsl +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep outsw +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep stosb +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep stosl +r#

x86_64 R-rd-phantom rep stosw +r#

# (b) LLVM returning a prefix as its own instruction -- CAUSE 2's family
# above, on encodings whose `df` token used to share the qualifier with the
# rest of the difference.
# --- x86_64: the residual the SIB sweep reaches.
#
# SIB.index and SIB.base were the last large pin in the x86 sweep.  The SIB
# byte used to be whatever the tail happened to spell -- `8d` on the SIB
# tail, `00` / `4d` / `44` on the others -- so the two registers a SIB
# address is computed from were sampled at four points out of sixty-four.
# Measured against the traced x86 population (mcf and perlbench, user and
# system, 1.64 G dynamic instructions, 47,512 distinct encodings): 4,240
# distinct encodings (8.92%) and 201,289,233 dynamic (12.25%) carry a SIB
# byte, and 99.13% of those distinct and 93.04% of that weight used a SIB
# byte other than the two the sweep had dedicated tails for (`8d` and `00`).
# index x base is now enumerated in
# full at every mod != 11 tail whenever the swept rm is 100; SIB.scale takes
# one value per tail so all four appear without quadrupling the population
# to measure a field that changes no register, no memory direction and no
# branch class.
#
# Cost: 171,048,960 -> 1,248,657,408 encodings, 11.9 s -> 85.6 s at
# --jobs=16.  It found no tracer defect and costs the 289 entries below.
# The reason that trade is taken here and the `66 2e` one is refused at the
# leg[] table in isaxcheck.cc is the RATIO, not the count: those 653 entries
# would have bought 0.00016% of dynamic weight, these 289 buy 12.25%.
#
# It is also the dimension that put `%riz` under the comparison for the
# first time.  Capstone renders the no-index, no-base SIB form as
# `(, %riz, 8)` and names `riz` in its register set; a pseudo-register
# reaching the dependency model would be a phantom edge in every trace.  It
# does not -- `--hex=8004e50000000011` gives boundary RD{riz}, LLVM RD{riz}
# and fields SRC{-} -- but until this dimension moved, nothing here could
# have said so either way.
#
# (a) APX REX2.  LLVM 18.1.3 decodes the `d5` prefix and the extended
# register file behind it; Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7 has no REX2 tables, and
# neither does QEMU's own x86 front end -- target/i386/tcg/decode-new.c.inc
# has no REX2 case at all -- so no `d5`-prefixed instruction can execute
# under the tracer, and the gap costs nothing until all three move.  This is
# the (c3) family above; the `26d5112233` line there was its one previously
# reachable member, and the swept SIB byte, which lands in the REX2 payload
# and opcode slots, reaches the rest.
#
# NOT CLOSABLE BY NARROWING THE SUBTARGET, which is the maintenance action
# the file header asks for first when LLVM and Capstone disagree about what
# exists: LLVM's X86 disassembler decodes REX2 unconditionally.  Verified --
# `--mattr=-egpr --hex=d5241000` still returns `adcb %r8b, (%rax)`, as does
# `--mcpu=core2`.  When a Capstone bump adds the tables these lines go dead
# and the gate turns red, which is the tripwire.

# (b) LLVM returning a prefix as its own instruction -- CAUSE 2's family
# above, new members.  The swept SIB byte lands where the next opcode would
# be, so it reaches prefix runs the fixed tails did not spell.  Capstone
# folds the prefix into the following instruction and LLVM stops at it,
# which leaves the whole of Capstone's answer standing opposite an empty
# one; the qualifiers are bookkeeping about which registers happened to be
# in it.

# (c) Zero-base segment override -- CAUSE 3's family above, on the VEX forms
# the swept SIB byte newly reaches.  `3e c5 ...` is a DS override in front
# of a two-byte VEX and the SIB slot supplies the VEX opcode byte, so the
# whole VEX 0F map arrives carrying an override.  In 64-bit mode DS has no
# base and contributes nothing to the effective address, so the boundary is
# right not to record a dependency and LLVM's segment operand is a
# modelling artifact.  FS and GS, which do have a live base, are recorded by
# the tracer and are not in this family.
x86_64 R-rd-missing vaddps +cs

# (d) TWO REX PREFIXES.  `44 4c 11 22` is a REX byte followed by another REX
# byte; the second immediately precedes the opcode and is the one that takes
# effect, so the instruction is `adcq %r12, (%rdx)`.  Capstone decodes it
# that way, QEMU agrees -- target/i386/tcg/decode-new.c.inc case
# `0x40 ... 0x4f` overwrites rex_r / rex_x / rex_b / vex_w and jumps to the
# next byte, so the last REX wins -- and Intel SDM Vol. 2 section 2.2.1 says
# a REX that does not immediately precede the opcode is ignored.  LLVM's
# MCDisassembler refuses the redundant prefix outright.  Capstone is the
# side that matches both the architecture and the emulator the trace is
# taken on, so there is nothing to repair.
#
# It is an EXACT entry and not a wildcard because `D-cs-only` is the
# subtarget-gap signal and one `*` there hides an ISA extension's worth of
# blindness.  The file header's condition for allowlisting a `D-cs-only` at
# all is that there be no LLVM feature left to enable, and there is none: a
# redundant prefix is not a feature.  Verify with `--hex=444c1122` (boundary
# decodes, LLVM rejects) against `--hex=4c1122` (both decode).
x86_64 D-cs-only lock adcq

# =====================================================================
# mipsel -- the FLOATING-POINT STATUS WORD the boundary names and LLVM
# does not (378 entries)
# =====================================================================
#
# WHAT: the boundary reports FCR31 (Capstone prints it `cop31`) or MSACSR
# as both a read and a write on every COP1 / MSA arithmetic instruction;
# LLVM MC reports neither.  Signatures `R-rd-phantom` / `R-wr-phantom
# <mnemonic> +cop#`.
#
# WHY IT IS NOT A TRACER DEFECT: the register really is read and written,
# and LLVM is the side that models nothing.  QEMU's update_fcr31() opens
# with SET_FP_CAUSE(env->active_fpu.fcr31, ...), a read-modify-write of
# the word (target/mips/cpu.h:83) executed on every call, then reads
# GET_FP_ENABLE(fcr31) to decide whether the exception traps;
# update_msacsr() is the same shape on env->active_tc.msacsr.  The
# architecture agrees: FCSR carries the rounding mode and the enables as
# inputs and the Cause and Flags fields as outputs of every arithmetic FP
# instruction.  LLVM's MIPS tables have no FP status register in any
# implicit list, which is also why a Capstone bump cannot close this --
# the gap is in the lineage, not in the decode.  See cap_mips_fp_ctrl_reg
# in disas/capstone.c for the class, its derivation from QEMU's call
# graph, and the exclusions (abs / neg / the moves / cvt.ps.s / fclass /
# fill) that are deliberately NOT listed here.
#
# HOW TO DELETE: if LLVM MC ever declares FCR31 / MSACSR on MIPS FP
# arithmetic these signatures stop appearing and the gate's dead-rule arm
# fails on them.  Spot-check with `isaxcheck --isa=mipsel --hex=00290646`
# (`add.s`): the `llvm` line must name the control register beside `f4`.
mipsel R-rd-phantom add.d +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom add.d +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom add.ps +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom add.ps +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom add.s +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom add.s +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom addr.ps +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom addr.ps +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom c.eq.d +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom c.eq.d +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom c.eq.s +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom c.eq.s +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom c.f.d +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom c.f.d +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom c.f.s +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom c.f.s +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom c.le.d +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom c.le.d +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom c.le.s +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom c.le.s +cop#

# =====================================================================
# mipsel -- the CP0 registers an instruction OWNS, and the CP0 state the
# exception it RAISES writes
# =====================================================================
#
# WHAT: the boundary reports a CP0 register on the TLB and CP0-control
# instructions, and on the instructions whose architectural effect is to
# raise an exception; LLVM reports none on either.
#
# WHY IT IS NOT A TRACER DEFECT: CP0 access is the entire architectural
# effect of these instructions, and QEMU's helpers name the registers one
# at a time -- r4k_helper_tlbr writes EntryHi/EntryLo0/EntryLo1/PageMask
# and reads Index (target/mips/tcg/system/tlb_helper.c:240),
# exception_return reads Status/EPC/ErrorEPC and clears EXL,
# helper_dvpe/evpe read-modify-write MVPControl.  LLVM's MIPS descriptors
# carry no implicit CP0 list at all.
#
# THE SECOND CLASS is the exception an instruction RAISES.  R7.6: a later
# `mfc0` of EPC, Cause or Status must wait on the faulting instruction's
# write, so that is an edge a renaming regfile has to respect and it is
# recorded.  syscall, break and the twelve conditional traps reach
# `set_EPC:` (tlb_helper.c:1420-1456), which read-modify-writes Status and
# Cause and writes EPC; sdbbp raises EXCP_DBp instead and reaches
# enter_debug_mode (:1204-1233), so it names Debug and DEPC on top and NOT
# EPC.  R4 covers the conditional members -- the write is a candidate
# whether or not the comparison fires -- and user mode, where it never
# happens, is the same inert case.
#
# THE TRAPPING ARITHMETIC joins that class, and the ISA draws the line
# itself.  add, addi, sub and their 64-bit siblings raise on signed
# overflow, and QEMU's gen_op_addr_add path takes EXCP_OVERFLOW to the same
# `set_EPC:` label with cause 12 -- the identical Status/Cause/EPC
# footprint syscall (8) and the traps (13) reach.  What makes it the
# opcode's own effect rather than a property of its operands is that MIPS
# ships each of these as a PAIR whose only difference is the trap:
# add/addu, addi/addiu, sub/subu and the three 64-bit pairs.  The `u`
# member is in neither this list nor the boundary's, which is the
# discriminator working.  `neg` is Capstone's spelling of `sub rd,$zero,rt`
# and appears here because the rows key on the mnemonic while the boundary
# keys on the instruction id.
#
# The line is drawn at instructions that RAISE, not at instructions that
# can FAULT.  A load's architectural effect is the load; the fault is a
# property of the address it computed.  Admitting it would put the
# exception file in the set of every memory instruction, and then of every
# instruction, since any of them can take a bus error -- a manufactured
# edge of exactly the class R8.1 forbids.
#
# CP0 is no longer one bucket: since the split these numbers resolve by
# register number into REG_SYSMMU, REG_SYSEXC, REG_SYSDBG and the residual
# REG_SYS, so the number the boundary names is the register QEMU names and
# the class is a consequence of it.
#
# HOW TO DELETE: if LLVM MC grows implicit CP0 lists for the first class
# the signatures stop appearing and the dead-rule arm fails.  The trap
# rows are the exception to that: LLVM will not start modelling MIPS
# exception entry, so they go only if the tracer stops recording the
# footprint, and R7.6 says it should not.  Spot-check with
# `--hex=02000042` (`tlbwi`), `--hex=18000042` (`eret`), `--hex=0c000000`
# (`syscall`) and `--hex=3f000070` (`sdbbp`, the row that names two
# classes).
mipsel R-rd-phantom deret +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom deret +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom dvpe +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom dvpe +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom eret +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom eret +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom evpe +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom evpe +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tlbinv +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tlbinvf +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tlbp +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tlbp +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tlbr +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tlbr +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tlbwi +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tlbwr +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom wait +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom add +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom add +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom addi +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom addi +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom break +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom break +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom neg +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom neg +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom sdbbp +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom sdbbp +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom sub +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom sub +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom syscall +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom syscall +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom teq +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom teq +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom teqi +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom teqi +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tge +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tge +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tgei +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tgei +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tgeiu +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tgeiu +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tgeu +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tgeu +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tlt +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tlt +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tlti +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tlti +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tltiu +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tltiu +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tltu +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tltu +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tne +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tne +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom tnei +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom tnei +cop#

# =====================================================================
# mipsel -- the MT ASE far operand's register FILE
# =====================================================================
#
# WHAT: on MFTR / MTTR the boundary reports the far operand in the file
# the (u, sel) pair selects -- CP0, DSP accumulator, DSPControl, FPR, FP
# control or COP2 -- where LLVM reports a GPR.  Hence the paired
# `+cop#`/`+ac#`/`+dsp`/`+f#` phantom and the `+r#` missing on the same
# encoding: one operand reclassified, not two disagreements.
#
# WHY IT IS NOT A TRACER DEFECT: the far operand is not a GPR, and
# reporting it as one both fabricates a dependency on a register the
# instruction never touches and deletes the one it does.  QEMU's gen_mftr
# / gen_mttr switch on exactly this pair (target/mips/tcg/translate.c:8077
# and :8301); binutils' mips-opc.c is the only static reference that names
# the file per form.  mftgpr / mttgpr (u=1, sel=0) genuinely ARE
# GPR-to-GPR, agree with LLVM, and are absent here -- the family is not
# corrected wholesale.
#
# HOW TO DELETE: if LLVM models the MFTR/MTTR operand classes these stop
# appearing.  Spot-check with `--hex=00200541` (`mftc0 $a0, $5`, must read
# a CP0 register) against `--hex=20200541` (`mftgpr`, must still read r5).
mipsel R-rd-missing mftr +r#
mipsel R-rd-phantom mftr +ac#
mipsel R-rd-phantom mftr +cop#
mipsel R-rd-phantom mftr +dsp
mipsel R-rd-phantom mftr +f#
mipsel R-wr-missing mttr +r#
mipsel R-wr-phantom mttr +ac#
mipsel R-wr-phantom mttr +cop#
mipsel R-wr-phantom mttr +f#

# =====================================================================
# mipsel -- the EVA unaligned loads merge into their destination
# =====================================================================
#
# WHAT: the boundary reports $rt as a READ on `lwle` / `lwre`; the sweep's
# LLVM side reports no read because the disassembled MCInst leaves the
# tied operand unmaterialised.
#
# WHY IT IS NOT A TRACER DEFECT: the read is real and LLVM's own
# MCInstrDesc carries TIED_TO for it -- only the MCInst drops it, the same
# shape as the plain LWL/LWR entries.  QEMU makes it literal: `case
# OPC_LWLE:` sets mem_idx and falls straight through into `case OPC_LWL:`,
# whose body opens with gen_load_gpr(t1, rt)
# (target/mips/tcg/translate.c:2128).  A partial write that keeps the rest
# of the register is a read-modify-write.
#
# HOW TO DELETE: if Capstone or LLVM materialises the tie, re-check with
# `--hex=1900a47c` / `--hex=1a00a47c`.
mipsel R-rd-phantom lwle +r#
mipsel R-rd-phantom lwre +r#


# =====================================================================
# x86_64 -- the system registers the boundary supplies and LLVM does not
# =====================================================================
#
# The boundary-layer face of the R7.2 / R7.5-x86 attribution; the
# fields-layer face is the matching block in isaxcheck_fields_allow.txt,
# and the two moving together is what says the plugin passed the facts
# through instead of inventing or dropping them.
#
# Twelve x86 system registers reach the decode as QEMU_PLUGIN_OP_SYSREG
# operands because Capstone's x86 register enum has an id for none of
# them.  Each register is one QEMU names in its own modelling of the
# instruction; the citations are at the switch in disas/capstone.c.
#
# THE TOKEN IS THE CLASS, NOT THE ARCHITECTURAL NAME.  A SYSREG operand
# enters the comparison as its role token -- `sysmmu`, `fcsr`, `sys`,
# `systimer`, `ssp` -- because that is what the dependency model
# schedules against (see the comment at the QEMU_PLUGIN_OP_SYSREG case in
# isaxcheck.cc).  So `stmxcsr` shows `+fcsr` here while LLVM spells the
# same register `mxcsr`: the two sides name one register in two
# vocabularies, and the pre-existing `R-rd-missing stmxcsr +seg` family
# is untouched by it.
#
# DELETION CONDITION, and it differs from its neighbours'.  LLVM's x86
# tables model no descriptor-table register, no MSR file and no TSC, and
# there is no reason to expect them to start; these rows expire if the
# TRACER stops recording the footprint, not because the reference catches
# up.
#
# THERE IS NO `+ssp` ROW HERE AND THAT IS NOT AGREEMENT.  An earlier
# version of this paragraph said the CET family agrees on `ssp` outright
# and offered the absent rows as proof the operands reach the comparison.
# They do not reach it: is_dropped_reg() in isaxcheck.cc drops the token
# `ssp` on BOTH sides for isa==ISA_X86_64, so this layer cannot see the
# x86 shadow-stack attribution in either direction.  MEASURED, 2026-08-23:
# with `saveprevssp` recording RD{ssp} WR{} against LLVM's RD{ssp}
# WR{ssp}, and `setssbsy` recording nothing at all against LLVM's
# RD{ssp} WR{ssp}, this arm still exited 0 with dead_allow_rules=0.  Four
# real attribution defects lived under that blindness (see the CET arm of
# the switch in disas/capstone.c).  The fields layer drops `ssp` from the
# REFERENCE side only, so the tracer's REG_SSP survives there and the
# rows in isaxcheck_fields_allow.txt are the only place this family is
# visible at all -- and only in the phantom direction.  BOTH DROPS EXIST
# because LLVM names `ssp` on every CALL, modelling a shadow-stack push
# QEMU does not perform; both delete when CALL's own shadow-stack
# footprint is ruled on.
#
#   +sysmmu     sgdt/lgdt, sidt/lidt, sldt/lldt, str/ltr.  gdt.base and
#               idt.base are stored and loaded in the translator
#               (target/i386/tcg/translate.c:3318, :3373, :3524, :3540);
#               helper_lldt / helper_ltr write env->ldt / env->tr and
#               READ env->gdt on the way (seg_helper.c:1288, :1342 and
#               `dt = &env->gdt` at :1304, :1359), which is why the lldt
#               and ltr rows carry the token on both sides.
#   +fcsr       ldmxcsr/stmxcsr and their VEX forms, fldcw/fnstcw,
#               fninit, fnsave/frstor, fxsave/fxrstor, ffree/ffreep --
#               env->mxcsr, env->fpuc, env->fptags[], which share the
#               FPCTRL role and therefore one token.
#               `R-rd-phantom fnstcw +fcsr` is a REFERENCE over-read
#               rather than our extra: LLVM and XED both model FNSTCW as
#               WRITING the x87 status word, which it does not
#               (fpu_helper.c:757 returns env->fpuc and touches nothing).
#   +sys        rdmsr/wrmsr/rdpmc, rdpid, rdtscp's second result,
#               xgetbv/xsetbv, the twelve XSAVE / XRSTOR forms and
#               setssbsy.  The MSR is selected at runtime by ECX, so one
#               token for the file is what a decode can honestly say;
#               IA32_TSC_AUX is an MSR and lands there for that reason;
#               XCR0 is read by every xsave/xrstor because
#               `rfbm &= env->xcr0` gates each component
#               (fpu_helper.c:2795, :3077).  setssbsy's is IA32_PL0_SSP
#               (MSR 0x6A4), the register `SSP := IA32_PL0_SSP` reads --
#               the only SSP-family term this layer can see at all, the
#               rest being dropped by is_dropped_reg() as described
#               above.
#   +systimer   rdtsc / rdtscp.  A counter that advances on its own is
#               its own dependency population.
#   xsave       `+r#,sys` is the decode-disagreement witness (Capstone
#               reads `xsave`, LLVM reads `ptwriteq`) with the new token
#               folded in -- the existing row repointed, not a new class.
x86_64 R-rd-phantom fnsave +fcsr
x86_64 R-rd-phantom fxsave +fcsr
x86_64 R-rd-phantom fxsave64 +fcsr
x86_64 R-rd-phantom lldtw +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom ltrw +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rdmsr +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rdpid +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rdpmc +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rdtsc +systimer
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rdtscp +sys,systimer
x86_64 R-rd-phantom setssbsy +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom sgdtq +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom sidtq +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom sldtl +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom sldtq +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom sldtw +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom stmxcsr +fcsr
x86_64 R-rd-phantom strl +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom strq +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom strw +sysmmu
x86_64 R-rd-phantom vstmxcsr +fcsr
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xgetbv +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xrstor +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xrstor64 +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xrstors +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xrstors64 +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xsave +r#,sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xsave +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xsave64 +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xsavec +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xsavec64 +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xsaveopt +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xsaveopt64 +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xsaves +sys
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xsaves64 +sys
x86_64 R-wr-phantom ffree +fcsr
x86_64 R-wr-phantom ffreep +fcsr
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fninit +fcsr
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fnsave +fcsr
x86_64 R-wr-phantom frstor +fcsr
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fxrstor +fcsr
x86_64 R-wr-phantom fxrstor64 +fcsr
x86_64 R-wr-phantom ldmxcsr +fcsr
x86_64 R-wr-phantom lgdtq +sysmmu
x86_64 R-wr-phantom lidtq +sysmmu
x86_64 R-wr-phantom lldtw +sysmmu
x86_64 R-wr-phantom ltrw +sysmmu
x86_64 R-wr-phantom vldmxcsr +fcsr
x86_64 R-wr-phantom wrmsr +sys
x86_64 R-wr-phantom xsetbv +sys

# =====================================================================
# riscv64: the boundary repairs of the RVV / Zfa / Zicsr arcs, which LLVM
# MC has no description for.  ADDED 2026-08-23, closing the gap COVERAGE.md
# 6.2 items 1-2 named: the repairs landed and this file was never extended,
# so the whole riscv64 arm had been failing rather than gating.
#
# EVERY BLOCK BELOW NAMES THE CODE THAT PRODUCES THE TERM AND THE AUTHORITY
# THAT VOUCHES FOR IT.  None of them is a comparison that passed: LLVM's
# instruction descriptions carry no implicit CSR access and no vector
# context at all, so these terms cannot appear on its side for any
# subtarget.  What DOES vouch for them is tools/implicit_audit.py, whose
# riscv64 expectations come from the Sail model.
# =====================================================================

# --- The RVV and FP context terms.  Five separate repairs meet in these
#     signatures, and a qualifier can carry several at once:
#
#   +v# on the READ side   an RVV vector DESTINATION is also a SOURCE.
#       Tail-undisturbed (vtype.vta=0), mask-undisturbed (vtype.vma=0) and
#       prestart (elements below vstart) each leave part of vd standing,
#       and all three are settled at runtime by state a preceding vsetvli
#       wrote -- so there is no encoding for which the read can be ruled
#       out.  Sail folds vd_val into init_masked_result before the element
#       loop; QEMU's vector helpers run `for (i = env->vstart; i < vl; i++)`
#       and then refill the tail from vd via vext_set_elems_1s().
#       Produced by the cap_riscv_is_vector_encoding() block in
#       disas/capstone.c.
#
#   +v# on either side     the nf group expansion.  `vlseg6e64.v v8, (a1)`
#       transfers six fields into six consecutive vector register groups
#       and Capstone names only the base (RVV v1.0 sec 7.8; Sail's
#       VLSEGTYPE loops read_vreg_seg over nf).  Without the expansion the
#       consumer of v13 never sees the load that wrote it.
#
#   +vctrl on the WRITE side   every RVV instruction CLEARS vstart.
#       RVV v1.0 sec 3.7: "the vstart CSR is reset to zero at the end of
#       execution of any vector instruction".  Sail closes every vector
#       execute clause with set_vstart(zeros()); QEMU writes
#       env->vstart = 0 at the tail of every vector helper.  vl, vtype and
#       vstart share QEMU_PLUGIN_SYSREG_VECCTRL, so they meet on one
#       generic slot and this is that slot's producer -- before it, an
#       instruction that trapped mid-vector resumed off a value the trace
#       said nobody wrote.
#
#   +fcsr on BOTH sides    the accrued exception flags.  fflags is
#       ACCUMULATED (`fcsr[FFLAGS] | flags` in Sail's accrue_fflags), so
#       the access is read-modify-write: an FP instruction depends on the
#       flags standing when it starts and leaves the union behind.  The
#       vector fixed-point saturation flag vxsat is the same shape.
#       Nothing reported it before -- so an fdiv.s that raised DZ and the
#       frflags that read it back had no dependency between them at all.
#
#   +frm                   the dynamic rounding mode an FP operation
#       encoded with rm = 111 reads.  Sail records it; LLVM models no frm
#       use.  It appears beside +fcsr rather than instead of it: the two
#       are different fields of the same word and the boundary now reports
#       both, which is why the bare `+frm` rows this block replaces went
#       dead -- their term is still there, inside a wider qualifier.
#
#   +vl,vtype              the vector configuration read.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom fadd.d +fcsr
riscv64 R-rd-phantom fadd.d +fcsr,frm
riscv64 R-rd-phantom fadd.h +fcsr
riscv64 R-rd-phantom fadd.h +fcsr,frm
riscv64 R-rd-phantom fadd.s +fcsr
riscv64 R-rd-phantom fadd.s +fcsr,frm

# --- Zimop reads NOTHING, and LLVM says it reads its operands.
#     An unimplemented May-Be-Operation is defined to write zero to rd and
#     read neither source.  QEMU says it in one line --
#     `gen_set_gpr(ctx, a->rd, ctx->zero)`, trans_mop_r_n / trans_mop_rr_n
#     in target/riscv/insn_trans/trans_rvzimop.c.inc -- and Sail marks the
#     parameters `_rs1` / `_rs2` unused.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc lists rs1/rs2
#     as operands and therefore as uses, which is a description of the
#     ENCODING, not of the effect.  Keeping LLVM's reads would fabricate a
#     dependency on registers the instruction never looks at.
#     Removable when LLVM stops modelling the unused operands as uses.
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.0 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.1 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.10 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.11 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.12 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.13 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.14 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.15 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.16 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.17 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.18 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.19 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.2 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.20 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.21 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.22 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.23 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.24 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.25 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.26 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.27 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.28 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.29 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.3 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.30 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.31 +r#
riscv64 R-rd-missing mop.r.4 +r#

# --- Zicfilp `lpad` reads x7 and consumes the landing-pad state.
#     A landing pad checks the label the indirect branch left in x7 (t2)
#     against the one in its own encoding, traps on a mismatch, and clears
#     the expected-landing-pad state the branch set.  ELP has no CSR
#     address of its own -- its architectural homes are the status word's
#     MPELP / SPELP fields -- so the state effect is named on mstatus and
#     lands on REG_SYS.  LLVM decodes `lpad` (experimental-zicfilp) but its
#     description carries neither the x7 use nor the ELP state, so the
#     whole of Zicfilp's forward-edge handshake is absent on its side.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom lpad +r#,sys
riscv64 R-wr-phantom lpad +sys

# --- Zacas `amocas.q` names two register PAIRS and LLVM names one register
#     of each.  The 16-byte compare-and-swap on RV64 takes an even-odd pair
#     for rd (low half in rd, high half in rd+1) and another for rs2; Sail
#     models the pairs directly (rX_pair_bits / wX_pair_bits).  Capstone
#     and LLVM both report only the even register, so without the repair
#     half the compared value and half the stored value are absent and the
#     high half of the memory word is produced by nothing.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom amocas.q +r#
riscv64 R-wr-phantom amocas.q +r#

# --- The read-only implementation constants (REG_SYSID) reached through
#     Zicsr: vlenb, mvendorid, marchid, mimpid, mhartid.
#     This is the riscv64 twin of the aarch64 rows bf1ee25247 added, and it
#     is the SAME sentence as the general `+sys` block above, following the
#     selector into its second class: LLVM carries the CSR selector as the
#     12-bit immediate the encoding spells, so no CSR the tracer records
#     for a Zicsr access can ever appear in LLVM's register sets, whichever
#     generic class it lands in.  These lines do not record a comparison
#     that passed; they record one that structurally cannot happen.
#
#     OBSERVATION, NOT COVERED BY THESE LINES AND NOT CLOSED BY THEM: the
#     boundary reports a WRITE to a read-only CSR for the store forms --
#     `--hex=731020c2` (`csrw vlenb, zero`) gives WR{REG_SYSID}.  On
#     hardware that encoding raises an illegal-instruction exception and
#     writes nothing, so a class whose defining property is having no
#     producer acquires one.  It is PRE-EXISTING and not introduced by the
#     IDENT split -- the identical shape is already allowlisted one block
#     up for REG_SYS (`--hex=731000c0` gives WR{REG_SYS}) -- and it is
#     invisible to this gate either way, because LLVM names no register on
#     that side to disagree with.  Recorded here so it is not mistaken for
#     something these lines vouch for.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrc +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrci +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrr +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrc +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrci +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrs +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrsi +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrw +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrrwi +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrs +sysid
riscv64 R-rd-phantom csrsi +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrc +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrci +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrrc +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrrci +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrrs +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrrsi +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrrw +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrrwi +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrs +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrsi +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrw +sysid
riscv64 R-wr-phantom csrwi +sysid

# --- R7.4: a CSR that decides whether an instruction TRAPS is a source.
#     A pending write to the gate must resolve before the instruction can
#     be said to do anything, which is an edge a renaming regfile has to
#     respect.  The gates named here are menvcfg (senvcfg at U, henvcfg
#     under H) for the CBO, WFI and Zicfiss enable bits and the FIOM bit
#     `fence` reads, and mstatus/mepc (sstatus/sepc) for the trap returns,
#     which also RESTORE privilege state and so write as well as read.
#     Sail puts every one of them in the encdec guard or at the head of the
#     clause.  LLVM's instruction descriptions carry no implicit CSR
#     access at all, so none of these can appear on its side.

# --- R7.4, continued: the privilege gate and the flush scope of a TLB
#     maintenance or hypervisor load/store.  Sail decides all three in the
#     clause head:
#       sfence.vma / sinval.vma   Illegal at U; at S when mstatus.TVM, at VS
#                                 when hstatus.VTVM (base_insts.sail:725-737).
#                                 The VMID that scopes the flush comes from
#                                 hgatp (get_vmid).
#       hfence.gvma / hinval.gvma mstatus.TVM (hext_insts.sail:183-189)
#       hfence.vvma / hinval.vvma hgatp.VMID, the flush scope
#       hlv / hlvx / hsv          hstatus.HU decides whether U may execute
#                                 them, hstatus.SPVP selects the effective
#                                 privilege the access runs at
#     Zicbop's `prefetch.*` is deliberately NOT here: every arm of its
#     translation is a no-op, so it cannot trap and its result depends on no
#     CSR -- the mstatus its Sail clause names is read only by the
#     translation of its own operand, which is out of scope on both sides.
#     LLVM's instruction descriptions carry no implicit CSR access at all, so
#     none of these can appear on its side.
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hfence.gvma +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hfence.vvma +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hinval.gvma +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hinval.vvma +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hlv.b +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hlv.bu +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hlv.d +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hlv.h +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hlv.hu +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hlv.w +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hlv.wu +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hlvx.hu +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hlvx.wu +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hsv.b +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hsv.d +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hsv.h +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom hsv.w +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom sfence.vma +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom sinval.vma +sys

riscv64 R-rd-phantom cbo.clean +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom cbo.flush +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom cbo.inval +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom cbo.zero +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom fence +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom mret +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom sret +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom ssamoswap.d +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom ssamoswap.d.aq +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom ssamoswap.d.aqrl +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom ssamoswap.d.rl +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom ssamoswap.w +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom ssamoswap.w.aq +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom ssamoswap.w.aqrl +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom ssamoswap.w.rl +sys
riscv64 R-rd-phantom sspopchk +sys

# --- R7.4 on AArch64: the FP / SIMD / SVE / SME enable gate is a source.
#     CPACR_EL1.FPEN, CPTR_EL2.FPEN and CPTR_EL3.TFP decide whether these
#     instructions execute or trap, so a pending write to one has to
#     resolve before any of them may proceed -- the edge R7 says a
#     renaming regfile must respect.  The boundary names all three (R4:
#     which gate applies is a runtime value, so a static set names every
#     candidate); the token is the CLASS, so they appear once.  LLVM MC
#     carries no legality axis, so this read is a phantom to it on every
#     row here and can never be anything else.  The 140 rows that also
#     read the FP control word or the ZA array were +fpcr / +za# before
#     this and are RE-POINTED above rather than added here.

# ctermeq / ctermne also carry the NZCV read Capstone omits and RMIF at
# the same revision reports -- see the CTERMEQ note in disas/capstone.c.
# LLVM MC models neither the flag read nor the enable gate.
aarch64 R-rd-phantom ctermeq +nzcv,sysfpen

# The AArch64 system-instruction group: SYS, SYSL and every alias of them
# (AT, DC, IC, TLBI, CFP, CPP, DVP, COSP, TRCIT, BRB, GCSPUSH*, GCSPOP*,
# GCSSS*), plus the encodings that take a system register as an operand
# without printing it (ADDG, SUBG, IRG, LDGM, STGM, STZGM, ERET*, ESB).
#
# The whole point of a SYS instruction is to produce system state, and
# the whole point of a SYSL is to consume it into Xt; neither is in the
# printed operand list, so before cap_aarch64_sysinstr_contract the
# entire group recorded only Xt.  LLVM MC models none of the system
# state either -- llvmWR{-} on every SYS, AT, DC, IC, TLBI, CFP, BRB,
# TRCIT and GCS form above -- so every one of these rows is the tracer
# recording state the reference does not.
#
# TWO of them are the other direction on the signature axis and are NOT
# a defect, they are LLVM being wrong about which way SYSL's Xt goes:
#
#   aarch64 R-rd-missing sysl +r#     LLVM makes Xt a Use
#   aarch64 R-wr-phantom sysl +r#     ... and Defs nothing
#
# `cstool -d aarch64 852329d5` shows Capstone carries the same error
# ("operands[0].type: REG = x5, operands[0].access: READ", "Registers
# read: x5"), which is why the boundary corrects it from the Rt field
# rather than from the access bits.  The architecture is unambiguous:
# sysl_rc_systeminstrs writes X[t], and the MRA-derived arc3 reference
# scores `sysl x5, #1, c2, c3, #4` as rD=GPR5.  A trace where the
# consumer of a SYSL reads an Xt no instruction wrote is the severance
# this closes, so the tracer is right and both legs of the reference are
# wrong together.
#
# Roles come from CRn:CRm (cap_aarch64_sysinstr_class), which is how the
# SYS space is organised: CRn 8 and the AT forms are MMU, the IC/DC
# forms are CACHE, TRCIT and BRB are DBG, the GCS forms are the
# shadow-stack pointer, and the prediction-restriction contexts stay
# residual.
aarch64 R-rd-missing sysl +r#

# THE ALWAYS-CONDITIONS.  LLVM MC gives every B.cond / BC.cond form the same
# condition-code Use, so `bc.al` and `bc.nv` arrive with `llvmRD{nzcv}` --
# and at cond<3:1> == '111' the architecture reads no flag at all.
# ARM DDI 0487C.a, shared/functions/system/ConditionHolds (J1-6366):
#
#     case cond<3:1> of
#         ...
#         when '111' result = TRUE;                 // AL
#     // Condition flag values in the set '111x' indicate always true
#     if cond<0> == '1' && cond != '1111' then result = !result;
#
# The '111' arm names no PSTATE field and the inversion below it is
# explicitly excluded for '1111', so NZCV is not an input to either
# encoding.  QEMU draws the same line -- trans_B_cond() emits a plain
# gen_goto_tb() with no arm_gen_test_cc() for 0xe and 0xf -- and
# disas/capstone.c's cap_aarch64_always_cond_contract() drops the read the
# reference mints, which is what makes this an `rd-missing` row rather than
# a silent agreement.  Removable if LLVM ever stops attaching the flags to
# the always-conditions; `cstool -d aarch64 1f000054` shows Capstone
# carrying the same claim, so the correction is not version-specific.
aarch64 R-rd-missing bc.* +nzcv
aarch64 R-rd-phantom addg +sys

aarch64 R-rd-phantom eretab +sysexc

aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +sysfpen

# --- The AArch64 system-register file, split by dependence behaviour.
#     cap_aarch64_sysreg_class used to answer OTHER for everything but
#     NZCV, FPCR/FPSR/FPMR, TPIDR_EL0/RO and 25 ID constants, so one class
#     token carried 1,182 architecturally distinct registers.  It now
#     answers the behaviour group, so MRS/MSR produce one signature per
#     group where they produced one in total.  LLVM MC models no system
#     register on these forms, so every group is a phantom to it exactly
#     as sys already was; the +sys rows stay live because 160 encodings
#     are still residual.
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +syscache
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +sysdbg
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +sysexc
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +sysmmu
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +sysperf
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrrs +systimer
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +ssp
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +syscache
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +sysdbg
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +sysexc
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +sysmmu
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +sysperf
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +systimer
aarch64 R-rd-phantom mrs +vctrl
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +ssp
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +syscache
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +sysdbg
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +sysexc
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +sysmmu
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +sysperf
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +systimer
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msr +vctrl
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +syscache
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +sysdbg
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +sysexc
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +sysmmu
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +sysperf
aarch64 R-wr-phantom msrr +systimer

#     the reasoning; SMCR is vector configuration, not the FP-enable gate.

# The EFLAGS repairs at the decode boundary (disas/capstone.c,
# cap_x86_eflags_from_bitmask / cap_x86_eflags_read_lost) restored flag
# dependencies Capstone published nowhere.  Where the restored access is one
# LLVM MC does not model, the difference surfaces here.  Each of the three
# classes below is a REFERENCE gap, not a repair to undo, and each names the
# QEMU code that performs the access.
#
# (1) The shift and rotate CL forms, SHLD/SHRD, IN/OUT and POPF read the
#     flags word, and LLVM MC's MCInstrDesc carries no implicit use of
#     EFLAGS for them.  Intel XED names every one.  QEMU:
#       * gen_shift_dynamic_flags() movconds cc_dst, cc_src AND cc_op on
#         (count == 0), selecting the OLD flag state at run time -- and
#         gen_shift_count() sets can_be_zero only for the CL form, which is
#         why only the CL forms are here;
#       * gen_POPF() calls write_eflags(T0, mask), a MASKED write, so every
#         bit outside the mask keeps its previous value;
#       * IN/OUT are gated on RFLAGS.IOPL, which QEMU hoists into
#         DisasContext (translate.c: dc->iopl = (flags >> IOPL_SHIFT) & 3)
#         -- a translator optimisation, not an absent dependency.
#     Deletable when LLVM MC gains the implicit use.
x86_64 R-rd-phantom inb +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom inl +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom inw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom outb +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom outl +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom outw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom popfq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom popfw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rolb +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom roll +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rolq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rolw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rorb +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rorl +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rorq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom rorw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom sarb +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom sarl +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom sarq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom sarw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shlb +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shldl +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shldq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shldw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shll +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shlq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shlw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shrb +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shrdl +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shrdq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shrdw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shrl +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shrq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-phantom shrw +flags

# (2) rcl / rcr with a segment override.  This is the SAME pre-existing class
#     as the file's other `+cs` / `+ds` / `+es` / `+ss` rows -- LLVM's X86
#     memory operand always carries a segment-register slot and Capstone does
#     not -- re-spelled only because the flags read these instructions really
#     do perform has now left the signature.  The rows it replaces
#     (`rclb +cs,flags` and friends) were deleted in the same commit.
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclb +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclb +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclb +es
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclb +ss
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcll +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcll +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcll +es
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcll +ss
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclq +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclq +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclw +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclw +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclw +es
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclw +ss
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrb +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrb +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrl +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrl +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrq +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrq +ds

# (4) THE ENCODED-ZERO-COUNT SHIFTS AND ROTATES, whose flag access LLVM MC
#     models and the MACHINE does not perform.
#
#     `shlq $0,%rax`, `rclb $0,%al`, `shldq $0,%rcx,%rax` and the rest of the
#     C0/C1 and 0F A4 / 0F AC space with an immediate whose masked value is
#     zero write NO FLAG and -- for the rotate-through-carry forms -- read
#     none either.  Intel SDM says so on every page of the family, in the
#     same sentence: SAL/SAR/SHL/SHR (Vol. 2B 4-687) "If the count is 0, the
#     flags are not affected"; RCL/RCR/ROL/ROR (4-543) "If the masked count
#     is 0, the flags are not affected", with RCL's Operation reading CF
#     inside a `WHILE (tempCOUNT != 0)` loop a zero count never enters;
#     SHLD (4-706) / SHRD (4-709) "If the count operand is 0, the flags are
#     not affected."
#
#     MEASURED ON THE MACHINE, not argued from the page.  A host probe
#     (exec102/hw/zcount.c) seeds EFLAGS with 0x000 and with 0x8D5, executes
#     one such instruction, and reads the word back: all NINE zero-count
#     forms leave every arithmetic flag exactly as seeded on both seeds,
#     while the three count-1 firing controls move.  That is the same
#     question PIN would be asked, asked of the part it models.
#
#     LLVM's MCInstrDesc says `Defs = [EFLAGS]` for the whole family because
#     a register allocator must assume the clobber -- the same
#     register-allocator convenience the CWD/CDQ/CQO entry above names, and
#     the same reason its `Defs = [AT]` on the MIPS FP branches is in section
#     1.  It is not a claim about execution and the machine contradicts it.
#
#     QEMU DRAWS THE SAME LINE: gen_shift_count() (target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc)
#     returns `*count = NULL` when `(decode->immediate & mask) == 0`, and
#     gen_RCL(), gen_RCR() and the shift emitters return on it having emitted
#     no op at all -- so QEMU's write list for these encodings is EMPTY, not
#     refused.  The boundary correction is
#     cap_x86_zero_count_shift_contract() in disas/capstone.c.
#
#     The `+cs,flags` / `+ds,flags` rows are section (2)'s segment-slot
#     difference seen on these same encodings, now that the flags have left
#     the tracer's side of the comparison; section (2)'s `+cs` rows still
#     fire on the CL and count-1 forms, which is why both spellings are here.
#
#     Deletable when LLVM MC models the count-0 case, which -- being a
#     run-time property of an operand -- MCInstrDesc has no way to express.
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclb +cs,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclb +ds,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclb +flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcll +cs,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcll +ds,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcll +flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclq +cs,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclq +ds,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rclw +flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrb +cs,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrb +ds,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrb +flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrl +cs,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrl +ds,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrl +flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrq +cs,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrq +ds,flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrq +flags
x86_64 R-rd-missing rcrw +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rclb +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rcll +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rclq +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rclw +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rcrb +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rcrl +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rcrq +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rcrw +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rolb +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing roll +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rolq +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rolw +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rorb +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing rorl +flags

x86_64 R-wr-missing rorw +flags

x86_64 R-wr-missing sarl +flags
x86_64 R-wr-missing sarq +flags
