# (3) pushq / pushw of %fs or %gs.  LLVM MC names NEITHER the segment source
#     nor the stack pointer for these two forms; Capstone names the segment,
#     and the boundary now adds back the rsp READ that Capstone drops on the
#     segment pushes alone (`pushq %rax`, `pushq (%rax)`, `pushq $0` and
#     `popq %fs` all report it).  A push computes its store address from the
#     current rsp, so the read is architecturally required.
x86_64 R-rd-phantom pushq +fs,r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom pushq +gs,r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom pushw +fs,r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom pushw +gs,r#

# FCMOVcc reads the flags it tests, and LLVM MC models no flag operand for
# the family at all.  The condition is the whole point of the instruction:
# without it a conditional move has no dependency on what set the condition,
# and Intel XED names RFLAGS a source on every one of the eight.  The
# harness's ADJ-5 settled the other half -- FCMOVcc does not WRITE the flags
# -- so the read is all that was missing.  The `+st#` rows these replace
# were deleted in the same commit; the st(i) half of the signature is
# unchanged and only the flags joined it.

# The leaf-dispatch instructions, whose whole dependency is a register
# convention that Capstone publishes nowhere: ENCLS / ENCLU / ENCLV,
# RDPKRU / WRPKRU, XABORT / XBEGIN and VMFUNC all arrive with no operands
# and no implicit registers.  The boundary restores Intel XED's sets
# (disas/capstone.c, cap_x86_implicit[]); LLVM MC models none of them
# either, so the repair shows up here as a phantom against LLVM.  The
# group-7 rows are prefix-gated, so these signatures are the UNPREFIXED
# encodings only -- 66 0F 01 CF is SEAMCALL and F3 0F 01 EE is CLUI, and
# Capstone misnames both.
x86_64 R-rd-phantom encls +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom enclu +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom enclv +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom vmfunc +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xabort +r#
x86_64 R-rd-phantom xbegin +r#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom encls +r#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom enclu +r#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom enclv +r#
x86_64 R-wr-phantom xabort +r#

# --- QEMU lowers the MIPS store-conditional onto tcg_gen_atomic_cmpxchg_tl
#     (target/mips/tcg/translate.c:2219-2244), which performs a REAL load
#     before the store, and the memop callback delivers it.  Capstone and
#     LLVM both report the ARCHITECTURE, where a store-conditional writes
#     and returns a status bit and does not read -- so the load arrived
#     with no declared slot and rendered as an orphan, `ld(0x410180)`,
#     while riscv64's `sc.d` (whose Capstone MEM operand happens to carry
#     READ|WRITE) rendered the same access matched.  The decode boundary
#     now declares the load on both ISAs; this is #177's decision applied
#     where it was owed -- the trace records what executed.  `ll` / `lr.d`
#     need nothing: neither reports the phantom store #177 removed.
#     `scd` is handled in the code and is NOT listed here: it is MIPS64 and
#     this sweep is 32-bit mipsel, so a row for it would be a rule that can
#     never match -- which is what the dead-rule detector is for.
mipsel M-extra-load sc
mipsel M-extra-load sce

# =====================================================================
# x87 IMPLICIT DATAFLOW -- the boundary carries it and LLVM MC does not.
#
# disas/capstone.c publishes the implicit half of every x87 instruction's
# register set, recomputed from the instruction bytes with QEMU's own
# gen_x87() dispatch key (mod, op, rm) -- see cap_x86_x87_effects.  Each
# access below is the operand set of the helper sequence QEMU emits:
# gen_helper_*_ST0 touches ST(0), gen_helper_fp_arith_STN_ST0 writes the
# named st(i), and gen_helper_fpush / fpop move env->fpstt, which is the
# TOP field of the status word.
#
# LLVM MC's x87 descriptions model almost none of it.  `fchs` carries no
# implicit operand at all in MCInstrDesc although it negates ST(0);
# `fadds (%rax)` names fpcw and the memory operand and neither ST(0) read
# nor ST(0) write; `fst %st(1)` names st1 as a USE where the instruction
# assigns it.  So the boundary reports a `phantom' wherever it carries a
# real x87 dependency LLVM omits, and 47 entries that used to sit above
# recording the OPPOSITE defect -- Capstone missing the status-word write
# -- are gone, which is the check that this block is the ratchet turning
# and not a hole being widened.
#
# HOW TO CHECK WHETHER THIS BLOCK CAN SHRINK: for any entry here, read the
# case in gen_x87() (target/i386/tcg/translate.c) that the encoding's
# (mod, op, rm) selects.  If the helper sequence there does NOT touch the
# register named in the qualifier, the boundary is wrong and the entry is
# a bug to fix.  The block disappears entirely if LLVM MC ever models the
# x87 stack in MCInstrDesc.
#
# THE READ OF TOP IS NOW CARRIED, which is why 69 `+st#` signatures became
# `+fpsw,st#` and 13 encodings acquired a `+fpsw` signature they did not
# have.  QEMU addresses the x87 stack through
# `#define ST0 (env->fpregs[env->fpstt].d)` (target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c),
# so EVERY form that names a slot reads env->fpstt -- the TOP field of the
# status word -- and an `fld` between two instructions makes the second
# one's `%st(0)` a different physical register.  cap_x86_x87_effects() adds
# X87F_SWR to any row carrying a stack bit, and states FCMOVcc and FFREE
# separately because their slot arrives as a Capstone operand rather than as
# one of those bits.  LLVM MC names no TOP read on any x87 form, so the edge
# lands here.
#
# DERIVED AND MEASURED, not asserted: x87_cw_derive.py (arc3_cov/x86_64)
# expands those macros -- unexpanded, the read is in no function body and
# the tool answers `no` on every x87 form -- and walks fpu_helper.c to a
# fixed point over an OBSERVED `-one-insn-per-tb -d op` dump.  Over its
# 153-encoding x87 denominator it says 140 read {fpus, fpstt, fptags} and 13
# do not, the thirteen being FLDCW, FNSTCW, FLDENV, FRSTOR, FXRSTOR, FNCLEX,
# FNINIT and the three 8087 NOPs -- none of which names a stack slot.  The
# boundary agrees with that derivation on 151 of the 153; the two it does
# not are FNOP and FWAIT, which read env->fpus for the EXCEPTION-SUMMARY bit
# rather than for TOP and name no stack slot, and are deliberately left out.
#
# THE CONTROL-WORD READ IS NOW CARRIED, and the rows it left behind are
# below rather than above.  X87F_CWR (disas/capstone.c) names FPCW as a
# SOURCE wherever QEMU's helper sequence consults it -- directly, on the
# five constant loads that switch on `env->fpuc & FPU_RC_MASK`, or through
# `env->fp_status`, which update_fp_status() fills from the rounding-mode
# and precision fields of the same word and merge_exception_flags() reads
# the masks out of.  245 `+fpcw` signatures became 16.
#
# WHAT SURVIVES IS THE OTHER DIRECTION, and it is LLVM's: MCInstrDesc
# attaches an FPCW use to the WHOLE x87 space, including the forms whose
# QEMU helper hands no float_status to anything and therefore reads no
# control word.  Those are pure bit-pattern moves -- FLD / FST / FSTP
# %st(i) (helper_fmov_*), FXCH (helper_fxchg_ST0_STN), FLD1 and FLDZ
# (floatx80_one / floatx80_zero, exact in every rounding mode), and the
# m80 pair FLDT / FSTPT (do_fldt / do_fstt copy the 80-bit encoding) --
# so `R-rd-missing fld1 +fpcw` and its eleven siblings say that LLVM
# models a read the machine does not perform.  `R-rd-phantom fbld
# +fpcw,fpsw` is the same disagreement with the arrow reversed:
# helper_fbld_ST0 DOES pass &env->fp_status to int64_to_floatx80, so the
# boundary names the read, and LLVM -- which names FPCW on `fildl`, the
# identical exact integer widening -- does not name it here.
#
# TO CHECK WHETHER ONE OF THESE CAN GO: read the helper the encoding's
# (mod, op, rm) selects in gen_x87() and look for `env->fpuc` or
# `env->fp_status` in it, resolving one level of call (fdiv_ST0_FT0
# reaches helper_fdiv, fprem/fprem1 reach fprem_common, fyl2x/fyl2xp1
# reach fyl2x_common).  If it is there and the entry says the boundary
# omits the read, the boundary is wrong and the entry is a bug to fix.
# =====================================================================
x86_64 R-rd-missing fnstenv +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing fnstenv +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing fnstsw +cs
x86_64 R-rd-missing fnstsw +ds
x86_64 R-rd-missing fst +fpcw,st#
x86_64 R-rd-missing fstp +fpcw

# R7.1-SCALAR: the destination of a UNARY legacy scalar SSE form is a PURE
# write.  ROUNDSS / ROUNDSD compute the low element from the source operand
# alone; the only part of the destination that survives is the upper lane,
# and a narrow write does not make a register a source -- rename does not
# know the data-width scope of the next reader.  LLVM MC models the
# preserve as a tied operand and so names the destination a USE; XED
# declares the same thing (`REG0=XMM_R():rw`) and the project's own
# reference arm already suppresses it under R7.1-SCALAR.  Capstone reports
# these two read-modify-write ALONE in their family -- `sqrtsd`,
# `cvtsd2ss`, `cvtsi2ss` and the rest of the unary set all come back
# WRITE-only on the same operand shape -- so leaving it would put a false
# RAW edge on every `roundsd` in a trace.  Removable when LLVM stops tying
# the operand, which is not expected.
x86_64 R-rd-missing roundsd +v#
x86_64 R-rd-missing roundss +v#

# =====================================================================
# CAUSE 3, THE `--srcenc` FORM.  ZERO-BASE SEGMENT OVERRIDE, scored against
# THE WIRE`S OWN SOURCE LIST rather than against the boundary`s decode.
#
# The class is the one already justified above for the `R-rd-missing` keys:
# LLVM`s X86 memory operand always carries a segment-register slot, so an
# explicit override makes LLVM report a read; in 64-bit mode the CS, DS, ES
# and SS bases are forced to zero and contribute nothing to the effective
# address, so QEMU computes no dependency and the wire is right not to carry
# one.  Intel SDM Vol. 1 sec. 3.7.4.1 and Vol. 2A sec. 2.2.1: in 64-bit mode
# the CS, DS, ES and SS segment-override prefixes are ignored; only FS and GS
# have effect.
#
# WHAT IS NEW IS THE SIGNATURE, NOT THE ARGUMENT.  The `--srcenc` arms score
# the wire`s published source list, which is QEMU`s ordered read list plus
# the survivor rows, and their read findings key on `SR-` rather than `R-`
# or `FR-`.  Nothing in the allowlist carried an `SR-` key at all, so this
# whole family was unallowed on eight arms that had already adjudicated it
# on the other side of the same tool.
#
# THE DISCRIMINATOR IS CHECKED AND HOLDS, and it is the reason this block is
# safe.  Every row below is `+REG_SEG0` (CS), `+REG_SEG1` (DS), `+REG_SEG2`
# (ES) or `+REG_SEG5` (SS).  NOT ONE is `+REG_SEG3` (FS) or `+REG_SEG4`
# (GS): measured over the whole eight-arm residue, every SEG3/SEG4 finding
# is in the PHANTOM direction instead -- `pushq %fs`, `rdfsbase`, `swapgs`,
# `maskmovq` -- where the wire reads the live-base register and LLVM`s
# MCInstrDesc has no operand for it.  So the family that would have been a
# real defect stays distinguishable from the benign one, which is the
# property the `+seg` -> per-register split was made to protect.
#
# ROWS WHOSE ADDED SET IS NOT PURELY A ZERO-BASE SEGMENT ARE NOT HERE.  A
# row reading `+REG_SEG0,REG_GPR#` names a general-purpose register too and
# is a different question; only rows whose entire difference is one of the
# four ignored segment registers are listed.
#
# Measured at exec103: 327 signatures, 69,375 encodings of the corpus.
# Removable when LLVM stops putting a segment slot on every X86 memory
# operand, which is not expected.
# =====================================================================
x86_64 SR-rd-missing bnd callq +REG_SEG0
x86_64 SR-rd-missing bnd callq +REG_SEG2

# =====================================================================
# THE SYSTEM REGISTERS THE REFERENCE HAS NO OPERAND FOR -- SEVEN ROWS,
# RE-DERIVED (exec112, PASS 64)
#
# WHAT THE DISAGREEMENT IS.  The wire names a system register as a source
# and LLVM MC names nothing there: `SR-rd-phantom <mnem> +REG_SYS*`, where
# the qualifier is the WHOLE difference set, so a row is licensed only when
# that register is the entire disagreement.
#
# WHY IT IS NOT A TRACER DEFECT.  LLVM models these registers as
# IMMEDIATES or does not model them at all -- a RISC-V CSR number, a MIPS
# coprocessor-0 selector, an x86 MSR index -- so its MCInstrDesc has no
# register operand to name.  `rdmsr` reads a model-specific register and
# LLVM's description says only that it writes EDX:EAX; `cbo.clean` operates
# on a cache block and LLVM names no CSR; `lldtw` loads the LDT register.
# The tracer records the architectural read.  R16: the dependency is the
# ISA's, and a reference that does not model the register cannot refute it.
#
# HOW TO CHECK WHETHER IT CAN BE DELETED.  Run the coverage census --
# `ISAX_DUMP_REGCOV=1 isaxcheck --isa=<isa> --check --srcenc=<corpus>` --
# and read the register's REGCOV line.  The row stands only while that line
# reads llvm_rd=0 llvm_wr=0 with the tag REFERENCE-HAS-NO-OPERAND.  Two
# other tags now exist and NEITHER justifies a row: UNANSWERABLE-NO-INDEX-KEY
# (the census cannot name the register at all) and UNANSWERABLE-TOKEN-DROPPED
# (the token is on is_dropped_reg()'s list, so the zero is this tool's own
# doing).  Measured at this tip, all four ISAs:
#
#   x86_64  REG_SYS 0/0/42,749  REG_SYSMMU 0/0/37,214  REG_SYSTIMER 0/0/7,108
#   riscv64 REG_SYS 0/0/10,009
#   mipsel  REG_SYSEXC 0/0/163,536  REG_SYSDBG 0/0/289  REG_SYSTIMER 0/0/128
#
# TWELVE ROWS THAT WERE PREPARED FOR THIS BLOCK ARE NOT HERE, AND THAT IS
# THE POINT.  All eleven aarch64 rows are REFUTED by the repaired census --
# LLVM names every one of those registers once the index is keyed off QEMU's
# own system-register names (REG_SYSFPEN llvm_rd=2,673; REG_SYSEXC 13,950;
# REG_SYSMMU 22,320; REG_SYS 24,687; REG_SSP 2,618) -- and x86_64 REG_SSP is
# UNANSWERABLE-TOKEN-DROPPED, which is FINDING 62-B's refuted row arriving as
# a measurement instead of an argument.  See arc3_cov/REFERENCE_COVERAGE.md.
#
# TWO OF THE SEVEN WERE RETIRED THE PASS AFTER THEY LANDED (PASS 66,
# FINDING 65-D).  A REGCOV line saying `llvm_rd=0 llvm_wr=0
# REFERENCE-HAS-NO-OPERAND` establishes that the reference cannot answer for
# the register.  It does NOT establish that a signature exists for a row to
# license -- and the qualifier here is the WHOLE difference set, so a row
# matches only where that register is the ENTIRE disagreement.  On mipsel
# REG_SYSDBG and REG_SYSTIMER never are: their census lines (0/0/289 and
# 0/0/128 above) are real, every one of those encodings disagrees about
# something else as well, and the two rows matched nothing on the day they
# landed:
#
#     DEAD isaxcheck_allow.txt:9575 mipsel SR-rd-phantom * +REG_SYSDBG
#     DEAD isaxcheck_allow.txt:9576 mipsel SR-rd-phantom * +REG_SYSTIMER
#
# The census-coverage test above is therefore NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT,
# and this note is the correction: a row also has to be reached.  The five
# that remain were re-measured at retirement time and each still matches.
# =====================================================================
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom * +REG_SYS
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom * +REG_SYSMMU
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom * +REG_SYSTIMER
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom * +REG_SYS
mipsel SR-rd-phantom * +REG_SYSEXC
